Observance dates come from bundled rules (`festivals.ts` / `festivalEngine.ts`) with a persisted per-city cache warmed off the interaction path. **Day selection is per-rule (`ObservanceRule.dayRule`, RULEBOOK §23):** absent ⇒ `udaya`, the tithi at sunrise, correct for the large majority; `chandrodaya` matches at **moonrise** and is what Sankashti Chaturthi and Karwa Chauth take, since the vrat ends with the moon sighting and arghya; `madhyahna` matches at the sunrise–sunset midpoint (Ganesh Chaturthi, Ram Navami); `aparahna` matches in the afternoon (sunrise + 0.7 × daylength) and is what **दर्श अमावस्या** takes, since पितृ तर्पण is an afternoon rite. Krishna Chaturthi typically opens mid-morning and closes before the next mid-morning, so sunrise matching named the day AFTER the night the moon is worshipped — Bhadrapada 2026 resolved to 1 Sep, whose 9:22 PM moonrise falls in Panchami, instead of 31 Aug, whose 8:39 PM moonrise falls in Chaturthi.

**Layer stack.** Calendar mode uses the parchment gradient plus a faint celestial chakra (`PanchangChakraBackground`, §48 asset family): a 360dp square at left −70 / top −110 with 0.18 opacity, clipped by the screen. The wavy sun is centered above the mode selector; rings, crescents/stars and leaf tips form a cropped upper-left ornament. Opaque content cards retain their surfaces. Vrat & Parv and Jyotish retain `panchang_celestial_almanac` via `BackgroundLayer`. Art is static, non-interactive and hidden from accessibility; it does not encode the selected date or chart. The content ScrollView and safe-area gutters retain their existing geometry.

**Structure (top to bottom):**

0. **Sky header** (`PanchangSky`, Panchang mode only; option A, Oct 2026) — a full-bleed gradient that starts **under the status bar** (the screen's `SafeAreaView` drops its top edge in this mode) with 28 pt bottom corners. It carries items 1–3 as glass controls (`skyInk(tone)`: `*Glass` fill, `*GlassBorder`, near-opaque `*GlassActive` + `saffron-deep` for the selected segment) and then a 132 pt **hero zone**. **Tone:** night — `skyNight*` stops, scattered `starLight` dots, cream `nightInk` type, light status-bar glyphs while focused — on a today view between sunset and sunrise (`useMuhurat().nowChoghadiya.phase`); day — `skyDay*` stops, two `cloud` wisps, `ink` type — otherwise, and always on a browsed date. **Hero zone:** by day, the right half retains the sunrise → sunset quadratic progress arc (travelled part solid, remaining part dashed, end dots and labels) and the 30pt painted eight-ray sun, breathing on a 6s loop that stops under reduce-motion; browsed dates park the sun at noon. At night the decorative moon is a fixed phase illustration, not a marker on a sunset → sunrise trajectory. `useMoonHorizon` defers four Astronomy Engine rise/set searches around the running night's actual sunset, including pre-dawn reads of yesterday's night, with a bounded exact-location/elevation/night cache. The painted moon appears only while the latest horizon event is a rise and the running Tithi is not Amavasya; before rise, after set, or when events are unavailable only the stars remain. Sunset and sunrise boundary labels are retained without a lunar path. A night-only intrinsically growing footer shows `चन्द्रोदय <time>` / `Moonrise <time>` and `अभी उदय नहीं` / `Yet to rise`, `चन्द्रमा क्षितिज के ऊपर` / `Moon above horizon`, or `चन्द्रास्त हो चुका है` / `Moon has set`. A short civil date is included when the rise differs from either the selected civil date or the night's sunset date, avoiding ambiguity after midnight and before dawn. Missing events use `—`, never an invented time. The moon is representative named-Tithi artwork; no altitude or trajectory is claimed. Day and browsed headers schedule no horizon solve. Horizon hills (`skyHills*`) close the zone. Left half, the **now hero** (`max(160, W/2 − 24)` wide): `अभी · चौघड़िया` / `Now · Choghadiya` eyebrow with a gold/avoid dot, the running choghadiya at 30 pt + a शुभ/त्याज्य chip — the kind word rides the eyebrow, not the name row, because beside the name it left ~30 pt in English and cut `Udveg` to `U.` and `Abhijit` to `Abhi…` (Oct 2026); the name is sized in JS (`fitChoghadiyaName`, shared with the strip: × system scale up to 1.3, capped so the script's widest name fits the hero width less the measured chip; `allowFontScaling={false}`) — the remaining-time line `41 मिनट शेष · 03:07 तक` (`formatSpan`, `panchang/muhuratSpan.ts`), and either `अगला शुभ <time> से · <name>` / `Auspicious from <time> · <name>` (time first, so a line that runs out cuts the name, never the time — `Next auspicious: Char from…` hid the answer; while त्याज्य — searched in the running half first, because before sunrise that half is last night's and today's solve does not hold it) or `फिर <next> · <quality>`. A browsed date shows a `शुभ मुहूर्त` / `Auspicious Muhurat` eyebrow over `अभिजीत` / `Abhijit` (30 pt × system scale up to 1.3, the row to itself) and its window instead. The hero is one accessible element with a spoken summary; the arc, stars and glyph are hidden from screen readers. The live read and its minute tick live inside `PanchangSky` (controls arrive as a `children(ink)` render prop), so the minute re-renders the sky and its controls — never the whole screen. **Share circle** (`action(ink)` slot): one 34 pt glass circle (`*Glass` fill, `*GlassBorder`, the `share` icon at 16 pt in `ink`) pinned to the hero zone's top-right (top 0, right 14), `accessibilityLabel` `Share panchang` — it shares the day as the पूर्ण पंचांग card (§33a). It sits in the zone, not in the item-2 chip row, because that row has no width to spare: a fourth circle there cut `Bengaluru` to 79% on a 390 pt phone and to nothing at 320 pt (rn-web width pass, 320–430 pt × hi/en/gu/kn). The day arc stays below the share control; the fixed night phase painting stays to its left.
1. **Surface modes** — the fixed first control in every mode: segmented pill `पंचांग · Panchang` / `व्रत-पर्व · Vrat & Parv` / `ज्योतिष · Jyotish` (13 pt, active `saffron-tint`/`saffron-deep`; glass on the sky). It must not move vertically when the selected mode changes — on the sky the header pads by exactly the status-bar inset plus the scroll's 8 pt (`SCROLL_TOP`), so it sits where it does in the other two modes.
2. **Contextual Panchang header** — shown below the mode selector (on the sky, below the day stepper) for Panchang and Vrat & Parv only. **On the sky** the row reads as the option-A preview drew it: the location chip and the calendar-system toggle share **one glass pill** (still two controls — the `Location: <city>` button and the Purnimant/Amanta radios — split by a 1 pt `*GlassBorder` rule, the toggle drawn `bare`), then the month button and My Vrat as 34 pt glass circles on the right. On the parchment (Vrat & Parv) it keeps the layout below; hidden in Jyotish because current city, lunar-month convention, and followed-vrat state do not apply to a birth chart. One compact row, equal-width flex sides so the centre toggle stays screen-centred:
   - *Location chip* (left): a drawn teardrop pin (11 px, `saffron`, counter-rotated `parchment-soft` hole — no emoji per §5) + city name at 12 pt, in a `parchment-soft` pill with `divider` border. Tap → Location Picker (below).
   - *Calendar-system toggle* (centre): segmented pill `पूर्णिमांत / अमान्त` (Purnimant default), active half `saffron-tint` + `saffron-deep`, inactive `ink-muted`. Persisted at `@vedansh:panchang-calendar-system`.
   - *My Vrat button* (right): 34 px circle, `gold` ★, with a `saffron` count badge when the user follows any vrat. → MyVrat.
3. **Day stepper + month grid** (Panchang mode; on the sky since Oct 2026, replacing the old calendar card's top half): one centred row — `‹` (`Previous date`, 44 pt), the **date block** (full date at 20 pt over one line `vara · masa paksha tithi` at 11.5 pt, shrink-to-fit 0.75 — e.g. `सोमवार · आश्विन शुक्ल पूर्णिमा`; its a11y label is the full date + the English identity line with Vikram Samvat; `testID="panchang-selected-date"`; tapping it also toggles the month grid), `›` (`Next date`). A horizontal swipe on the row steps the day. A small glass **`आज`** pill (`Today`) appears centred under the row **only once the day has moved off today** — on today it would do nothing (the smoke flows assert it after `Next date`). The **month button** (`MonthGlyph`, a11y `Expand calendar` / `Collapse calendar`, `*GlassActive` while open) sits at the right of item 2's row, beside My Vrat. The **month grid** opens as its own card under the sky (`parchment-soft`, `divider`, `radii.lg`, `elevation.card`): month header with `‹ ›` (`Previous month` / `Next month`), the muhurat-overlay chip when present, weekday row, and per-week rows of date cells (selected `saffron-tint`, today `gold` ring, overlay days `gold-tint` ring, `व्रत`/`पर्व` tags) — picking a day folds it away.
4. **Day panel** (the panchang proper, once computed — an `ActivityIndicator` in `saffron` while the day is derived off the render path):
   - *No standalone date header* (removed Aug 2026): the vara · date · संवत् · paksha line lives as the sky's date subtitle (item 3 above), so the day panel starts with the observance ribbon (when there is one) and the Muhurat card.
   - *Observance ribbon* (`ObservanceNowRibbon`, Oct 2026): when the selected day has an observance, a ≥ 56 pt `cardActiveFrom` row with a `goldChipBg` border (`radii.lg`, `elevation.card`) leads the day panel, above the Muhurat card — the selected day is represented once here, above its timing details. A 40 pt `skyNightTop` disc holds the tithi's moon (`CelestialGlyph`, 24 pt), then the rule name (15 pt title face) over `<deity> · कथा पढ़ें` (12 pt `ink-muted`; कथा only when the rule carries one), a `+N` `saffronTint` chip for further observances, and `›` → `ObservanceDetail`. **Pre-dawn carry-over:** on today before sunrise the Hindu day is still yesterday's, so the ribbon shows the previous civil date's observance as `<name> की रात्रि` / `<name> night` over `<deity> · सूर्योदय H:MM तक` — a nishita Sharad Purnima or a moonrise Karwa Chauth is still the one running at 2 AM, though the civil date has turned. The pre-dawn check is read per render, not ticked. Renders nothing on a day without an observance.
   - *Muhurat card* (`MuhuratGlanceCard`, PRD-14; reworked Oct 2026): the `cardActiveFrom → cardActiveTo` gradient card leading the day panel (under the sky and ribbon, **above** the anga grid). The sky answers "what is running now, and for how long"; this card lays out the rest of the day's timing. Kicker row (Aug 2026): the `आज का मुहूर्त` eyebrow (`cardLatin` for en; script-bold serif, no tracking, for hi/gu/kn — Cormorant has no Indic glyphs, §3) on the left, and the **running tithi** right-aligned opposite it — a 10 pt `saffron-deep` `तिथि · ` eyebrow tag + the tithi name at 14.5 `ink` (title face) + a quiet 11 pt `ink-soft` `तक H:MM` end instant when this day's solve knows it (`formatEndInstant` short-date suffix on past-midnight ends; the तक line takes the script body face outside English — Cormorant has no Indic glyphs, §3). Baseline-aligned with the eyebrow, shrink-to-fit to 0.8. Beneath that line, when the sunrise tithi hands over **within the same civil day**, a quiet 10.5 pt `ink-muted` **handover line** — `फिर <tithi> — शेष दिन` / `then <Tithi> — rest of day` (`successorTithiToday()` in `panchang/prevailingTithi.ts`, pure). It is a second line, not an extension of the first, so neither has to shrink to fit both. `तक 08:51` alone states when the day's label stops being true and never what is true for the remaining fifteen hours — the gap that made a Chaturthi vrat look like it belonged to the next date the almanac heads चतुर्थी (Aug 2026 report). The helper returns null — never a misleading name — when the tithi runs past the next sunrise, when it ends after midnight (the तक line already carries that date), and on kshaya days (the tile renders both tithis already); it is also suppressed once a today surface's live kicker has itself moved on to the successor. The tithi is the one calendar fact promoted into this first-viewport hero (the anga grid it belongs to sits past the fold on most phones), and on a today surface it is **live**: `prevailingTithi()` (`panchang/prevailingTithi.ts`, pure) walks sunrise tithi → kshaya tithi → successor-by-index on the minute tick, so past the end instant the kicker names the tithi actually running now — the successor renders name-only because its end belongs to the next day's solve, never a guess. Browsed dates keep the sunrise (udaya-vyapini) tithi. Renders only once the day's solve lands (the skeleton keeps a bare eyebrow); the anga Tithi tile below stays the canonical sunrise-tithi + kshaya detail. Then the **choghadiya strip** (`ChoghadiyaStrip`): the eight periods of the half that holds now (`useMuhurat().nowRun` — last night's before sunrise; a browsed date shows its day run with nothing marked) under a 14 pt `रात्रि के चौघड़िया` / `दिन के चौघड़िया` heading, as equal ≥ 44 pt segments, each a 9 pt bar (`choghadiyaShubh` / `choghadiyaAvoid`) over its 10.5 pt name (one line, sized in JS by `choghadiyaNameSize`: 10.5 pt × the system text scale up to 1.3×, then capped so the script's widest name — अमृत / Shubh / અમૃત / ಉದ್ವೇಗ, measured in em — fits the row's measured segment width; `allowFontScaling={false}` and **no `adjustsFontSizeToFit`**. On iOS new-arch auto-fit ignores `minimumFontScale` and floors at 4 pt, and the shipped 10.5/15 auto-fit label collapsed every name to ~3 pt on the live today strip while browsed dates looked fine, Oct 2026; §3.0); past segments at 45 % opacity; the running one 13 pt tall with its elapsed share filled (`gold` / `avoidDeep` over the `*Track` remainder) and a 6 pt `saffron-deep` notch at now. It **replaces the old 4 pt progress strip**; the running segment's a11y label carries the percent elapsed. Under the bars, the half's start and end clocks with a `शुभ` / `त्याज्य` swatch legend between them. Tapping a segment states it in a detail row — name · compact window · `अभी` / `बीत चुका` / `आगे` · शुभ/त्याज्य pill (`accessibilityLiveRegion="polite"`); the running segment shows by default, and a pick resets when the run rolls over (the strip is keyed by its first start). Then a two-up `राहु काल` / `अभिजीत` tile pair as **equal halves tinted by quality** (`avoidTint` + `avoidChipBg` border / `goldTint` + `goldChipBg` border, each with a 7 pt dot and a label — never colour alone), the range via `formatRange` (24-hour, so `07:39 – 09:07` never wraps) at 15 pt shrink-to-fit, and on a today view a `5 घं 13 मि में` / `अभी चल रहा` / `बीत गया` state line; and a `सभी मुहूर्त व चौघड़िया →` footer → `MuhuratDetail`. While its own `useMuhurat` solve is in flight it shows a **skeleton** in the same gradient card (kicker text + muted `divider`/`parchment-soft` placeholder bars sized to the strip, tile pair, and footer, `accessibilityRole="progressbar"`), so the section reserves its footprint instead of popping in below the day panel. The solve comes from the shared **`panchangDayStore`** (per `locationKey` + calendar system, keyed by absolute civil date, persisted — §60), so revisiting a date, re-mounting the card, a day the Muhurat Finder already swept, and a cold app start all render instantly with no skeleton; only the live "now" read recomputes each minute. `useMuhurat` owns no cache of its own — `MuhuratDay`/`nowPeriods` are re-derived per call because they are pure arithmetic over the three cached days. **Times, ranges, and the quality chip use the non-italic semibold/bold Cormorant face, never the thin italic `cardLatin` (§3), and the chip sits on `avoidChipBg`/`goldChipBg` so it reads as a solid pill on the gradient (§12); avoid-chip text uses `avoidDeep` — the tint composites darker than the card, dropping raw `avoid` under AA (§2).** Every clock on the sky, card and strip sets `fontVariant: ['lining-nums', 'tabular-nums']` — Cormorant's default old-style figures made `3:07` and `9:41` read as two typefaces in one card (Oct 2026).
   - *Anga grid, uniform 2×2*: Tithi · Nakshatra · **Nitya Yoga** · Karana each render on identical painted-gradient tiles (`panchangTileFrom → panchangTileTo`, `panchangTileBorder`, `radii.lg`, `elevation.card`, 10 pt vertical / 14 pt horizontal padding, 8 pt gap) — one size, no prominent/secondary split. Each tile: a **12 pt** `saffron-deep` type label (tracked uppercase Cormorant in English; plain script serif otherwise — the §3.0 floor, raised here with the Oct 2026 slim-cards pass), the value in the active reading language only at 21 pt `ink` (single-line, `adjustsFontSizeToFit` down to a 0.8 scale so the longest name — "Uttara Bhadrapada" — fits without truncation), and `till HH:MM` at 15 pt when the anga ends that day. No second cross-script line. End instants that fall past midnight carry a short-date suffix (`तक 02:04, 12 जुल` — `formatEndInstant` in `panchang/muhuratFormat.ts`, shared with the Muhurat card) so a next-day end never reads as this morning; panchang convention shows end times only (an anga's start is the previous one's end, usually on the previous day). On **kshaya** days — a tithi or nakshatra that begins after this sunrise and ends before the next, touching neither (e.g. Ekadashi on 10 Jul 2026) — the Tithi/Nakshatra tile adds a second row: the skipped anga's name at 17 pt `ink` plus its own `तक` line, so the day reads `दशमी तक 08:16 · एकादशी तक 05:22, 11 जुल` instead of Ekadashi silently vanishing between Dashami and Dwadashi. Data: `PanchangData.kshayaTithi` / `kshayaNakshatra` (engine-detected via the sunrise-to-sunrise index jump). The **Tithi tile alone** carries the same 12 pt `ink-muted` **handover line** as the glance-card kicker (`फिर <tithi> — शेष दिन`, `successorTithiToday()`) under its `तक` line, on the identical null rules — the tile headline stays the sunrise (udaya) tithi the almanac names the day by, and the handover says who holds the rest of it. No other anga carries it. **The yoga tile is labelled नित्य योग / Nitya Yoga, never bare योग** (PRD-27, RULEBOOK §24): the 27-cycle Sun+Moon yoga — one of which is literally named सिद्धि — must stay distinguishable from the शुभ योग card directly below it.
   - *Vaas cards, 2×2 slim grid* (Oct 2026 "slim cards" pass, `components/DayVaasCards.tsx`): four equal cards directly under Nitya Yoga · Karana (`panchangTileFrom → panchangTileTo`, `panchangTileBorder`, `radii.lg`, `elevation.card`, 10 pt vertical / 14 pt horizontal padding, 8 pt gaps) — they replaced the tall यात्रा (a 104 pt compass) + अग्नि वास·हवन pair, whose height pushed the sunrise card below the fold. Each label has a 30dp painted icon, 6dp before it: `gayatri`, `chandra`, `vastu-disha`, `agni-vaas`. The heading and chip wrap within the card on narrow or enlarged-text layouts. दिशा शूल alone uses `panchangAvoidFrom → panchangAvoidTo` and `panchangAvoidBorder`. Each card mirrors the anga-tile shape: a header row (label left at **12 pt** `saffron-deep` title face, and its **chip pushed to the right of the same row** when it has one — the chip rides the header instead of a fourth line, so the card stays as short as an anga tile), a value at **19 pt** `ink` (devanagari-bold title face, `adjustsFontSizeToFit` 0.8), and — only when it adds something the label does not — one context line at **13 pt** `ink-muted`. Chips are 11.5 pt, 2×9 padding, fully rounded (`radii.pill`). Row 1 — **सूर्य**: value `<sun rashi>` (e.g. कन्या), and **no caption** (the dropped `सूर्य राशि` only repeated the label). **चन्द्रमा**: header chip = a **gold chip** (`goldChipBg` / `ink-soft`) listing the favourable travel directions `<d1>, <d2> शुभ` (e.g. पश्चिम, दक्षिण शुभ); value `<moon rashi> · <चन्द्र वास direction>` (e.g. मिथुन · पश्चिम); caption `चन्द्र वास`. Row 2 — **दिशा शूल**: header **avoid chip** (`avoidChipBg` / `avoidDeep`) `यात्रा टालें`; value `<shool direction>` (e.g. पूर्व) `avoidDeep`; caption `<vara> — इस दिशा की`. **अग्नि वास**: header **avoid chip** `हवन वर्जित` **only when havan is barred**; value `<agni vaas>` (e.g. पाताल); caption `तक <time>, फिर <next>` (or `आज पूरे दिन` when it holds). Each card is its own a11y element with a spoken summary; colour never signals alone — every chip carries a word (`यात्रा टालें`, `हवन वर्जित`, `… शुभ`). Rules: चन्द्र वास direction by rashi element (fire पूर्व · earth दक्षिण · air पश्चिम · water उत्तर); the favourable-travel pair = the चन्द्र वास direction itself (Moon ahead/सम्मुख) + the one that puts it on the traveller's right (`chandraVaasFavourableDirections`); अग्नि वास `(tithi from शुक्ल प्रतिपदा + 1 + vara from रविवार) mod 4` → 0/3 पृथ्वी, 1 आकाश, 2 पाताल; दिशा शूल from the Muhurat Finder's `DISHA_SHOOL_BY_VARA`. **The दिशा शूल / चन्द्र वास overlap is deliberately not reconciled in code** (when a शूल direction is one the Moon marks शुभ) — Chandra-vasa is out of v1 pending the `docs/roadmap/conventions/muhurat-lagna-v1.md` content review (PRD-16 §14 Q4). Pure derivation over the solved day (`panchang/vaas.ts` → `dayVaas` / `vaasTiles`; Moon rashi from `PanchangData.moonRashi`, Sun rashi from the new `PanchangData.sunRashi` — the Sun's sidereal rashi at sunrise, added to the engine solve, `PANCHANG_DAY_CACHE_VERSION` 6) — nothing on the scroll path calls the engine.
   - *शुभ योग card* (`ShubhYogaCard`, PRD-27 — §69): directly under the anga grid, **only on days a shubh yoga forms** — zero chrome otherwise (present-or-absent is the entire vocabulary; no empty state, no "no yoga today" copy). In Panchang mode, a painted-gradient `PanchangDaySurface` with a 22dp More `star` before the `शुभ योग` eyebrow; Finder callers retain the plain variant. Then, then one row per window — the shared `MuhuratChip` (yoga tone: `goldChipBg` + `saffronDeep`, full "… योग" name) with the window range right-aligned (Cormorant SemiBold for en; the script body face otherwise, because `formatEndInstant`'s past-midnight short-date suffix is Devanagari — the anga-tile face rule). Windows run nakshatra-to-nakshatra via `useShubhYoga` → `computeShubhYogas` (store-backed, no private cache); the 26:12 extended-hour style is never used.
   - *Times card*: 2×2 grid — Sunrise, Sunset, Moonrise, Brahma Muhurta — each a 40dp painted `surya` / `sunset` / `moonrise` / `aarti` icon + the unchanged 10pt label and Cormorant SemiBold 13pt value. The enclosing card uses `PanchangDaySurface`.
5. **Following-day observances** (`PanchangNextDayCard`): selected-day observances stay in the top ribbon. `useObservancesForDate(addDays(selectedDate, 1), calendarSystem)` supplies all following-day rows, respecting the current city, calendar system and active regional lenses. No observance means no card or empty-state copy. Each row is the same `ObservancePreviewCard` / `ListingCard variant="observance"` used in the latest Vrat listing: 78dp square `ObservanceArtwork`, the shared cream catalog frame, intrinsically growing bilingual title, and independent 44dp save/reminder target. The row's countdown slot says `कल / Tomorrow` on today and `अगले दिन / Next day` on browsed dates; tapping the body opens `ObservanceDetail`. The bell uses `useVratReminderAction`, including the shared first-use sheet and saved-preference reuse. Sankashti keeps its published Ganapati form and Angarki suffix through a display-title override; artwork, saves and reminders retain the original rule identity. Failure falls back to the rule name. Existing catalog `ObservanceCard` reading/katha/vidhi actions and day notes remain on the Vrat surface.
6. **Regional and Pitru entry points**: retain the non-dismissable `LensDiscoveryRow` on a selected day with no observance when calendars are available, plus the conditional `PitruPakshaDayChip` and private `PitruSmaranDayChip`. Their removal was an unresolved owner choice in the handoff and is not included. Seeded-calendar feedback retains its dismissal and now uses the painted More `regional` icon; the discovery row uses the same painting.

**Day-panel icon and surface contract (D0, 28 October 2026).** `PanchangDaySurface` supplies the 160° `panchangTileFrom → panchangTileTo` gradient, 1dp `panchangTileBorder`, `radii.lg`, and warm `elevation.card`. Avoid surfaces use their three parallel tokens. Anga icons are at top-right with an 8dp inset: Tithi's 32dp `skyNightTop` disc contains the 26dp painted, phase-aware `CelestialGlyph`; the other three use 36dp `nakshatra`, `nitya-yoga`, and `karana`. Nakshatra retains its six-star painted constellation with strengthened sepia outlines and connectors for legibility at this size. The label reserves 30dp on its right and at least 34dp vertically so values and next-day end times do not run under the art. Vaas, yoga and times keep their existing copy and values. `chandra` is a plain cratered lunar disc and `moonrise` the same subject at the horizon; neither has a star or emblem. The three other new lunar/solar subjects remain distinguishable at 30dp. Utility controls use the shared glyph family (including the header's saved star); live celestial phase marks remain calculated. The six additions have their own 300KB asset budget, without changing the existing icon budgets. Exact prompts, retained masters, export rules, reference/master/shipped hashes and contact sheet are in `docs/evaluations/panchang-day-icons-2026-10-27/`.

**Shared painted Sun/Moon (28 October 2026).** `CelestialGlyph` reuses the retained `chandra` and `gayatri` PNGs; there are no new bitmap assets or stars beside the moon. The full moon painting is normalized to its reviewed 40–472px visible frame and shaded with `moonPaintedShade`. `utils/moonPhase.ts` constructs the lit spherical limb with a semicircle and elliptical terminator: Shukla tithis wax with the right limb lit, Purnima (index 14) shows the entire painting without shade, Krishna tithis wane with the left limb lit, and Amavasya (index 29) has no bright limb. These are representative named-Tithi illustrations, not an exact continuous ephemeris. The tile keeps its sunrise Tithi; the ribbon keeps the Tithi supplied by its selected-day solve; the live night sky follows `prevailingTithi(...).index` on the existing minute updates, including handovers and kshaya links. The header's day/night selection, arc position, halo breathing and reduce-motion behavior remain as before. Browsed days retain the day sky and sun at noon. Full/rising Moon symbols in other informational slots retain their existing meanings. Verification: `CelestialGlyph.test.tsx`, `moonPhase.test.ts`, `PanchangSky.test.tsx`, and `panchang-celestial-phase-smoke.yaml`; native full/waxing/waning/dark captures are in `docs/evaluations/panchang-day-icons-2026-10-27/celestial-phase/`.

**Regional coverage of the observance catalog** (Sept 2026 — the “Goga Navami is missing” audit). The catalog is **174 rules, 162 of them default-visible**; the rest are `advanced`/`regional` and reachable only through catalog search. Two rules about what goes where:

- **A regional observance that a whole state keeps is `default`, not `regional`.** `visibility: 'regional'` currently means *invisible everywhere* — `getObservanceCatalog()` filters to `default` and there is no surface that opts back in — so tagging Gangaur or Chhath “regional” would have hidden them from the users who actually keep them. The identity lives in the rule's **`shortDescription*` and `searchTerms`**, which name the state, the mela and the local name (the pattern `bhadwa-chauth` set): a Tamil user sees one extra dot on a day, a Rajasthani user sees their festival. The two rules that remain `visibility: 'regional'` (`karthigai-vrat`, `rohini-vrat`) are the monthly Krittika and Rohini nakshatra series, awaiting the opt-in surface designed in `docs/roadmap/prds/42-regional-parv.md`. Wave 2 gave both a **correct** rule (they were `catalog-only` *and* carried a 1-indexed nakshatra, so they resolved to nothing twice over) without making them visible: twelve rows a year each is exactly the universal-day load that PRD's lens exists to prevent. The Tamil date's default-visible home is the annual `karthigai-deepam`; `rohini-vrat` is knowingly still the only home of its own date, and is the Jain lens's job.
- **A shipped katha is not a shipped date.** Five Rajasthani observances shipped a full bilingual katha while their rule was `catalog-only` — no `lunarMonth`/`tithi` at all — so they could be *read about* but never appeared on a calendar day: **गणगौर, सकट चौथ, शीतला सप्तमी, बछ बारस, आशा दशमी**. Adding a katha entry must never be mistaken for adding the observance.
- **And the converse — a shipped date is not a shipped story.** तेजा दशमी shipped as a dated rule (Bhadrapada S10) with no katha behind its detail page, so the day named वीर तेजाजी and then had nothing to read. It now carries `teja-dashami-katha` — a six-part bilingual retelling (खरनाल · पनेर · जलती झाड़ी का नाग · लाछा की गायें · वचन का पालन · तांती और मेला) that reaches the Observance Detail katha card, the Katha Library tile and जिज्ञासा's *Read katha* action with no screen change. Drik indexes no Tejaji katha, so the catalog entry cites the same Rajasthan tourism portal the rule does — the §11.1 folk-deity carve-out, now closed over katha sources too (RULEBOOK §23a.2).

**Wave 1 additions (17 rules).** Rajasthan — गोगा नवमी (Bhadrapada K9, Gogamedi), रामदेव जयंती (Bhadrapada S2, Ramdevra), तेजा दशमी (Bhadrapada S10, Parbatsar), बछ बारस (Bhadrapada K12), गणगौर (Chaitra S3), शीतला सप्तमी (Chaitra K7), शीतला अष्टमी · बसोड़ा (Chaitra K8), दशा माता व्रत (Chaitra K10), सकट चौथ (Magha K4, `chandrodaya`), आशा दशमी (Ashadha S10). Bihar/Mithila — चैती छठ (Chaitra S6), मधुश्रावणी (Shravana S3), सामा-चकेवा (Kartika S7), चित्रगुप्त पूजा (Kartika S2). Pan-Hindu gaps the same audit surfaced — कार्तिक पूर्णिमा (Kartika S15; Dev Deepawali, Pushkar and Sonepur melas), चैत्र नवरात्रि प्रारंभ (Chaitra S1; also Gudi Padwa / Ugadi / Cheti Chand — Ram Navami had shipped for years with no opening day), महावीर जयंती (Chaitra S13; default-visible on the same reasoning as Buddha Purnima). Each rule cites its source in `festivals.ts` and is pinned to a **published** civil date in `observanceDates.test.ts`; `verify:observances` re-derives all but `sakat-chauth` independently.

**Wave 2 additions (16 rules, Sept 2026)** — the universal gaps PRD-42 Appendix C listed as belonging to *everyone*, plus the first Tamil/Malayalam observances the engine can express. Universal — रथ सप्तमी (Magha S7; Surya Jayanti), रंग पंचमी (Chaitra K5; Indore's gair), शनि जयंती (Jyeshtha Amavasya, the Vat Savitri day), राधा अष्टमी (Bhadrapada S8 — Appendix C called it “the largest single universal gap left”), हनुमान जयंती · कार्तिक (Kartika K14; the Telugu–Tamil reckoning, a **second** rule beside the Chaitra one per §23.9), गोपाष्टमी (Kartika S8), चंपा षष्ठी (Margashirsha S6; Khandoba/Subrahmanya), सोमवती अमावस्या (the amavasya that lands on a Monday — 1–3 a year), विश्वकर्मा पूजा (Kanya Sankranti; a solar rule, not an alias, because the observance is its own). Tamil/Malayalam — कार्तिगई दीपम (Krittika in Vrischika), थै पूसम (Pushya in Makara), पंगुनि उत्तिरम (Uttara Phalguni in Meena), वैकासि विशाकम (Vishakha in Vrishabha), चित्रा पूर्णिमा (Purnima of the **Mesha solar month**, which is *not* Chaitra Purnima — 1 May vs 2 Apr in 2026), अवनि अविट्टम (Shravana Purnima; the upakarma), कर्किडक वावु (Amavasya in Karka; Kerala's tarpana day). Every one is pinned to a **published** civil date in `observanceDates.test.ts` — exactly, not ±1.

Two new mechanisms carry them, both narrow: a real **`nakshatra`** matcher (the day whose sunrise a nakshatra covers, with the same first-of-two and kshaya handling the tithi matcher uses), and two optional **constraints** on an existing rule — `solarMonth` (sidereal solar month, 0 = Mesha) and `weekday` on a lunar-tithi rule. Constraints rather than new rule types: they filter days a rule already claimed, so they can never move a rule off its tithi.

**Held back, and why.** **Onam**, **Thrissur Pooram** and **अट्टुकाल पोंगाल** are absent although the solver now exists: all three are Kerala pooram-class observances, and the two that can be checked resolve exactly one day late under the sunrise rule that is correct for all four Tamil rules (Thrissur Pooram 27 vs 26 Apr 2026; Attukal Pongala 4 vs 3 Mar). Two independent rules failing in the same direction is a **convention** difference, not a solver bug, and PRD-42 Open decision 6 says that belongs in a convention doc rather than being guessed at. **आडि पूरम** is held for a different reason: a solar month can contain Pooram twice and the almanacs take the second (14 Aug 2026, not 18 Jul), which `recurrence: 'annual'` cannot express — and sources themselves disagree, so it is a §23.9 two-rules case.

**Section A additions (27 rules, Sept 2026)** — found by a sweep of the standard pan-Hindu festival list (Drik, the Sri Mandir calendar) after a user asked why वराह जयंती was missing. All universal, all plain lunar-tithi rules, no engine work. Vishnu's avatars — मत्स्य (Chaitra S3), वराह (Bhadrapada S3), वामन (Bhadrapada S12), कल्कि (Shravana S6), हयग्रीव (Shravana S15) जयंती, and हलषष्ठी · ललही छठ (Bhadrapada K6, Balarama's day). Saints — सूरदास and आदि शंकराचार्य (both Vaishakha S5), तुलसीदास (Shravana S7, links to the Ramcharitmanas reader), वाल्मीकि (Ashvina S15, links to the Valmiki Ramayan reader), वल्लभाचार्य (Vaishakha K11), कबीर (Jyeshtha S15). Devi and sampradaya days — बगलामुखी, धूमावती, अन्नपूर्णा, नर्मदा, जानकी जयंती, महेश नवमी, फुलेरा दूज. Named days — नरक चतुर्दशी (now a card of its own, not only the दीपावली arc's gap label), काल भैरव जयंती, मौनी अमावस्या, गणेश जयंती (`madhyahna`), भीष्म अष्टमी (`madhyahna`), माघ पूर्णिमा, and both गुप्त नवरात्रि openings (Ashadha, Magha). **Varaha, Matsya, Kalki and Hayagriva carry `dayRule: 'aparahna'`**, because Drik fixes these days by an afternoon puja window. That matters in 2026 for two of them: Varaha is 13 Sep, not the sunrise 14th, and Hayagriva is 27 Aug, a day before Raksha Bandhan on the same Purnima. Each rule is pinned to a published date and to the sibling rule that shares its tithi (`observanceDates.test.ts`, SECTION_A_*). Sixteen forward-year dates (2027–2028) are pinned too (SECTION_A_FORWARD), and `VERIFY_YEARS=2025-2031 npm run verify:observances` re-derives every row independently through 2031 with 0 wrong-month results. The default year grows from 308 to 335 rows, about 0.08 more observances a day. **Held back:** कूर्म जयंती and छिन्नमस्ता जयंती (Drik keeps both at the evening/sayahna hour. In 2027 the tithi opens after 4 PM on the published day, so every day rule the engine has names the next day; they ship with the pradosh/sayahna rule, PRD-42 E-1), ललिता पंचमी (2025 published a day before the sunrise Panchami), शारदीय दुर्गा अष्टमी / महानवमी (published sources split 18/19 and 19/20 Oct 2026), वैकुंठ चतुर्दशी (nishita-vyapini, not modelled), बलराम जयंती (three dates across three traditions), गायत्री and रामानुज जयंती (contested tithi), and सर्वपितृ अमावस्या (already on the calendar through Pitru Paksha). **Enrichment (in progress):** गणेश जयंती now carries a full katha (`ganesh-jayanti-katha`, the king and Queen Ratnavali, plus a closing section that sets it apart from भाद्रपद's गणेश चतुर्थी and माघ कृष्ण's सकट चौथ) and its own til-gud bhog profile (`ganesh-jayanti-bhog`, kept separate from `ganesha-bhog` so the til offering doesn't show up on Ganesh Chaturthi). A second batch followed: नरक चतुर्दशी (`narak-chaturdashi-katha` — Narakasura's end and King Rantideva — plus `narak-chaturdashi-bhog`, which names Bengal's choddo shaak as regional), वराह and वामन जयंती (their own avatar kathas and bhog profiles: the kalash-and-coconut daan, the 52 pedas and curd-rice daan), मौनी अमावस्या (`mauni-amavasya-katha`, a mahatmya of the amrit kumbh, Manu and the silent snan; bhog reuses `pitru-offering`), and काल भैरव जयंती (reuses the Bhairava-origin `masik-kalashtami-katha` and `kalashtami-bhog`). The other 21 still carry only their short description; each gets katha and bhog as it is sourced.

**Sankranti day rule corrected.** `findSolarFestivalDate` returned the civil day whose *midnight followed* the ingress instant rather than the day *containing* it, so **all twelve sankrantis resolved one day late** — Makar Sankranti 2026 as 15 Jan against a published 14 Jan, Kanya Sankranti as 18 Sep against 17 Sep. Found because `vishwakarma-puja` rides Kanya Sankranti and inherited the shift. The loop now compares each day's midnight with the *next* day's and returns that day. Twelve shipped dates move one day earlier, onto their published dates; `CACHE_VERSION` 6 forces already-scanned cities to re-scan, and `observanceDates.test.ts` pins Makar/Mesha/Kanya against published almanacs plus the ascending-order invariant across all twelve.

**Both amavasya rows.** An amavasya lasts about a day, so the tithi at sunrise and the tithi in the afternoon can name different dates, and both are published. The app therefore ships **two** rules on Krishna 15, exactly as Drik does: **अमावस्या व्रत** (`amavasya-vrat`, udaya — the snan-daan day, and the day the Tithi tile itself labels अमावस्या) and **दर्श अमावस्या** (`darsha-amavasya`, `aparahna` — the पितृ तर्पण / shraddha / fasting day). They coincide in 64 of the 99 lunations from 2024–2031 and differ by exactly one day in the other 35; on a coinciding day the list shows both rows, the same way पूर्णिमा व्रत and श्री सत्यनारायण व्रत already do. Drik names them differently — the aparahna row is "Darsha Amavasya" every month, the udaya row carries the lunar month ("Bhadrapada Amavasya", "Ashwina Amavasya") — and both rows are pinned against its published 2026 list: 10 Sep / 11 Sep (the reported case: amavasya runs from 10:33 AM for the rest of the 10th), 8 Nov / 9 Nov, and 10 Oct where the amavasya covers both instants and the two coincide. Retagging the single existing rule was rejected — it would have moved 35 shipped dates. सर्वपितृ अमावस्या (`pitruSmaran.ts`) is aparahna-assigned since Oct 2026 (§63, RULEBOOK §23.13) and falls on the दर्श अमावस्या day in every year 2024–2031 (29 Sep 2027, where अमावस्या व्रत is 30 Sep).

**Two-tradition rows and the published-rule days (Oct 2026, RULEBOOK §23.14–23.18).** Where Drik prints a Smarta and a Vaishnava date, the app does too: every named Ekadashi has a hidden-from-browse twin **वैष्णव <name>** (`VAISHNAVA_EKADASHI_RULES`) and Janmashtami has **जन्माष्टमी (वैष्णव)** (`janmashtami-vaishnava`). Each twin resolves ONLY on a day its Smarta row does not (about one Ekadashi in five; Janmashtami 2023/2025/2029), so an ordinary day shows one card, never two identical ones. The twin renders as a normal `ObservanceCard` on its day — same katha, upvas and food guidance as its Smarta row, a description that says why it is a day later — and is reachable from search; it is not a second entry in the Vrat/Parv browse list (`getRulesForCategory`). Days are decided by the published multi-clause rules in `panchang/specialDayRules.ts`: Ekadashi by Dharmasindhu's sunrise-pattern table (Smarta can be the second Ekadashi sunrise or the Dashami-sunrise day), Janmashtami by Nishita + Rohini (Smarta 2025 is 15 Aug; 16 Aug is the Vaishnava row), Raksha Bandhan and Holi by the Bhadra rules (Rakhi 30 Aug 2023 / 23 Aug 2029 / 2 Aug 2031; Holi 8 Mar 2023 / 4 Mar 2026), and every aparahna rule by span cover. 121 published New Delhi dates 2023–2031 are pinned in `observanceDates.test.ts`.

**Evening rule.** `bachh-baras` is worshipped at the evening godhuli/pradosh hour and Drik publishes it pradosh-vyapini (7 Sep 2026); it carries `dayRule: 'pradosh'` since the Sept 2026 day-rule audit (RULEBOOK §23.12), which also moved Diwali, Dhanteras, Dussehra, Maha Shivaratri and the monthly Pradosh / Masik Shivaratri / Kalashtami / Purnima / Skanda Sashti vrats onto their published days. The same audit added **महा नवमी** (`maha-navami`, Ashvin Shukla Navami, `madhyahna`, `dot` marker, deity मां सिद्धिदात्री) — held back earlier because 2026 sources split; Drik's own dates 2024–2029 now pin it, and it often shares the Durgashtami day (11 Oct 2024, 26 Sep 2028). It is not a Sharad Navratri arc member and not in the festive-reminder catalog.

**No new notification family.** These rules are **not** added to the default-on festive-reminder catalog (§38): that list is “famous enough that every user is already observing it”, and a state festival is not. The opt-in path is the existing ★ follow → vrat reminders, which now works for all seventeen because they finally resolve to a date.

**Muhurat Finder door** (`MuhuratFinderDoor`, PRD-16 §60) — the regular `ListCard` (§8) with the painted `muhurat` thumb (40dp in the 52pt `CardThumb`), title `शुभ मुहूर्त खोज` over `Find a Muhurat`, meta `गृह प्रवेश · वाहन · नामकरण…`, and a नया/NEW badge top-right; inserted **between the glance card and the anga grid**: "is now auspicious?" readers are the users with a date decision to make. It shares the glance card's gradient (the earlier flat variant was removed, Oct 2026), so a **16pt top margin** keeps the two from reading as one card. It pushes `MuhuratFinder` in the same Panchang stack; nothing above or below it moves.

**Muhurat Detail** (`MuhuratDetailScreen` → shared `MuhuratCardBody`, reached from the glance-card footer — and, for a specific chosen day, from the Muhurat Finder day detail and Abujh rows, §60) — the gold-॥-framed panchang card (§5): panchang + sun rows, then the full 8+8 day/night choghadiya table and अभिजीत/राहु/गुलिक/यमगण्ड rows. The panchang rows use the same `formatEndInstant` next-day suffix as the anga tiles, and kshaya days add `क्षय तिथि` / `क्षय नक्षत्र` rows so the card never disagrees with the tiles. The yoga row is labelled **नित्य योग** (the PRD-27 collision rule, §69), and — full variant only, like the नित्य योग/करण rows themselves — a **शुभ योग** row per present yoga follows it (`<full name> · <window>`; end-only when the window opens at sunrise, start–end on a mid-day onset; derived per render from `computeShubhYogas(p, md.nextSunrise)`, never cached). Each is a quality-tinted `Muh` row (`goldTint` auspicious / `avoidTint` avoid) with the currently-running period ring-bordered in `saffron` + an `अभी` badge. **Name and time render in dark `ink`/`ink-soft` on both qualities** — the tint plus a small signal-coloured `· शुभ/त्याज्य` text tag carry the quality (§12, never colour alone); the text is never itself tinted-down (terracotta-on-`avoidTint` was muddy ~4.8:1). The quality tag, time range, and now-badge use the **non-italic semibold Cormorant face, never the thin italic `cardLatin`** (§3) — the same readability fix as the glance card. The card title (top bar + `MuhuratCardBody` heading) is **`आज का पंचांग` only when the date is today** (`useMuhurat().isToday`); a specific finder/abujh-selected day reads **`इस दिन का पंचांग`** so it never claims to be today while the dateline shows a different day. The top-bar share circle (`ShareButton`, `पंचांग साझा करें`) shares the **पूर्ण पंचांग card** (§33a) for the screen's date — the same image the sky header shares — not a capture of this body — through the §39.10 provider, so it gets the Daily Bhakti picker (Instagram post/story, card-language chips, Send as text). The screen's earlier private capture passed pixel sizes that iOS reads as points, giving a ~2040×6000 PNG that WhatsApp refused with "This item cannot be shared" (Oct 2026).

**Catalog view** (`व्रत-पर्व` tab; landing layout completion, 26 October 2026): the mode switch, location, calendar system and search retain their existing order. The catalog uses the neutral parchment background; its selected mode pill is maroon `catalogAccent` with `onPrimary` text. The calendar/Jyotish sky treatment, festival ribbon and Choghadiya strip remain unchanged. Search gains a magnifier, `catalogSurface`/`catalogBorder`, and the correct script font.

**Today** is the approved split hero: an existing subject painting occupies a 36%-width, 190pt-high panel on the left, with the category, 18/28 title, deity, two-line 13/21 authored description and reading actions on the right. `ObservanceTodaySummary` keeps a separate ≥44pt expand/collapse button; the full description remains present and readable on expansion. Reading/Katha/Vidhi actions remain independent sibling controls, each ≥44pt tall. Day notes, complete source prose, occurrence-specific names and navigation destinations are retained. Cards grow with enlarged system text; there is no fixed card/text height. The personal-ledger bar preserves all three destinations and live state. It uses `ListingCardFrame tone="catalog"`, three equal columns, top-aligned18pt icons, centered14/22 labels and bottom-aligned status lines. Its height grows when labels or state wrap.

**Upcoming** retains the next six future observances and excludes today. Its horizontal `ObservancePreviewCard` tiles are 155pt wide with a full 141×72 inner banner above complete wrapping titles and date/countdown metadata. Reminder bells remain separate 44×44 controls; only the card content opens detail. **Browse by type** uses `CatalogCategoryTile`: the same two-column Vrat/Festivals/Upvas/Katha destinations and counts, followed by full-width Puja Vidhi. Cards have a quiet chevron, ≥88pt content-driven height, script-safe metadata, and painted subjects at 60pt (44pt below 360pt screen width). Search results and regional-calendar additions retain their merged behavior.

**Illustrated slots and shared cards.** Vrat, Parv, Upvas, search results and My Vrat render `ObservancePreviewCard` over the Kids Stories `ListingCard` shell. Katha and followed muhurats use the same illustrated `observance` variant. Its 78pt square artwork, unclamped bilingual title/caption and inline date/countdown grow with their content; the inner press target must not use vertical `flex: 1`/zero basis. Upcoming cards are 155pt wide with a 141×72 banner filling the inner width (1pt borders and 6pt padding). The independent save/reminder bell overlays the top-right of the banner. This follows the user’s explicit full-banner clarification; it supersedes the earlier 110pt image beside a blank star column. Their6pt rail gaps, cream catalog surface,14pt corners and shallow shadows follow the prototype. The rail snaps at card-plus-gap boundaries and derives trailing padding from its measured width so the final visible group is complete too; horizontal padding gives shadows room beyond each card. The calendar title uses maroon `catalogAccent`. The shared frame’s default reading tone is unchanged. Their body uses intrinsic height (`flex: 0`). Save/reminder and open controls are independent 44pt sibling targets; My Vrat reserves a second gear target for editing timing. Katha's typed `granth` category resolves the actual scripture painting. All 200 rule assignments use the same painted calendar art direction: complete square Ahoi/Tulsi/Siddhidatri replacements, shared scenes for joint subjects, and neutral scripture/ritual paintings for traditions without one deity. Twenty-six small whole-canvas copies of existing approved paintings paint immediately offline, alongside the dedicated local portraits/scenes; calendar cards never transition through old symbolic icons. Today retains its illustrated expandable introduction and separate reading actions. Category doors use 64×44 paintings, grow with their labels, and become full width above system fontScale 1.2. Search fields grow with system text. The standard 92pt / 4:5 Kids Stories card remains unchanged. Artwork coverage, exact Image Gen prompts/masters/hashes, thumbnail source hashes and native evidence are in `docs/evaluations/vrat-consistency-2026-10-27/`; the earlier reference rebuild remains historical evidence. The full-width banner correction is documented in `docs/evaluations/vrat-full-banner-2026-10-27/`. The earlier geometry and six saturation corrections are documented in `docs/evaluations/vrat-palette-layout-2026-10-27/`; review palette contact sheets as well as deity semantics before accepting calendar artwork.

**Observance List** (`ObservanceListScreen`): category drill-in over the Home gradient, sorted by the existing engine's next occurrence. Shared `ReaderHeader` retains Back, bilingual category/count masthead and listing share. Content groups into civil-month headings, including the year for future years; unresolved dates use an Other observances group. `ObservancePreviewCard` rows pair a 64pt painting with the complete primary name, other-language caption, date/countdown and regional tags. Text wraps; cards have no fixed height. Dates still come from `getNextOccurrence` and the chosen calendar system; countdowns compare civil days rather than elapsed hours. The **44×44 trailing reminder bell is a sibling of the detail Pressable**, never nested: `gold` filled/selected means saved, outline means available to save. A saved preference with both notices Off uses bell-off. The first row and bell retain their existing feature-tour refs. Search matches names, deity and area names. Sharing uses precisely the currently visible results.

The single horizontal **area/saved filter** (`AreaFilterChips`) retains All, Pan-India and only the regions present in the category. A `★ मेरे व्रत / My Vrat` chip filters by the same follow store as My Vrat/reminders, including categories without regional tags. Saved, area and query filters compose; All resets the saved and area selections. Removing the last saved match shows an explanatory empty state with a route back through All. Non-catalog callers retain the existing optional-area behavior. Existing 44pt chip targets, script-aware fonts and language support remain.

**Artwork identity and availability.** `panchang/observancePortraits.ts` makes an explicit subject decision for every default-visible observance. `ObservanceArtwork` uses actual deity/sage paintings rather than enlarging the symbolic `StoryIcon` attributes (Gauri’s flower, Ganesh’s modak, Shiva’s trident). Twenty-five existing published painting sources are reused through the shared R2 cache, with source-specific portrait centres; complete reading masters remain outside the bundle. Thirty-five new 256px square subject thumbnails live in `assets/icons/observance-portraits/` (952,574 bytes total), with exact prompts, hashes and subject review in `docs/evaluations/vrat-deity-artwork-2026-10-26/`. Composite identities retain every named person through adjacent portrait slices or a dedicated shared scene. Traditions and practices without one deity retain neutral ritual/scripture art. A published painting’s symbolic loading/offline fallback is replaced after the cached image loads; new thumbnails are bundled. These cropped calendar thumbnails do not alter any reader/story art or its frames. Row portraits remain 64pt, Today is 76pt and Upcoming is 80pt. Browse category symbols and global Home/navigation icons retain their established meanings. Original-prototype fidelity and editorial acceptance are separate from technical/subject checks.

**Area tags are display-only** (`panchang/observanceAreas.ts`). `OBSERVANCE_AREAS` maps a rule id to the regions its own description names; a lensed rule takes its lens as its area. A tag **never hides a rule**. Gangaur tagged `rajasthan` is still on every user's calendar, and the filter narrows only the list being viewed. Hiding stays the lens's job (§73, RULEBOOK §23a.5). The area ids are the 22 lens ids, so names come from the same lazily loaded registry. `observanceAreas.test.ts` checks that every tag names a real default-visible rule, that no tagged rule also carries a lens, that सर्वत्र plus the tagged rows adds up to the whole list, and that every chip it offers is non-empty.

**Observance Detail** (`ObservanceDetailScreen`) — hero (category pill + deity + a **क्षेत्र pill** for a tagged rule (1 pt `divider` outline, 11 pt `saffron-deep` area names; the tag row wraps and centres at phone width), name at 24 pt centred, other-language caption, and a `saffron-tint` "अगला · Next · date · in N days" pill), then an action row: **Add reminder / Saved to My Vrat** (bell with outline `saffron` pill; fills `saffron` with `parchment` text when saved — uses the same first-use/reuse flow as the cards, §38) and **॥ Read Katha** (filled `saffron`). Following shows a transient (3.5 s) `gold-tint` "Added to My Vrat — View →" bar. For rules inside a **festival arc** (PRD-28 — `ganesh-chaturthi`/`anant-chaturdashi`, `navratri-start`/`dussehra`, the four Diwali days) the **पर्व-अर्क strip** (§65.2) sits directly under the ask row; every other rule renders nothing there. Below: **महत्व · About** prose, a katha card, and — **last on the page** — the single **"How to observe" home** (PRD-09 Phase 4 §65 composed with PRD-19 Phase 2B), in four states keyed off a verified upvas entry (`upvasId` → `getUpvasInfo`, verified-only) and a resolving `vidhiId`: **(1) upvas only** → `उपवास विधि · How to observe` heading + the §65 fast-facts panel; **(2) vidhi only** → exactly the shipped `पूजा विधि · How to observe` block, unchanged — the same card shell as the katha card (`parchment-soft`, `divider`, `radii.lg`, `elevation.card`) with a saffron ॥ glyph, the vidhi's bilingual title, a `N चरण · लगभग M मिनट` meta line, and › — tap → `VidhiDetail` (§62) carrying the next occurrence's `dateMs` so the samagri checklist keys to the right festival date; **(3) both** → the `उपवास विधि` heading, fast facts first, and the vidhi card beneath them inside the same panel, its meta line prefixed `पूजा विधि · ` so the card keeps its own identity; **(4) neither** → no section at all. There is never a "coming soon" placeholder here — the parent PRD-09 §6.2 placeholder slot is retired. The top bar's right slot is the §39 share circle (`Share this day`): a one-scope prose card with the name, the next date as its header, and only the verified content this screen itself shows — deity, the fast's rule, bhog offerings, the katha's opening paragraph (§39.4).

**My Vrat** (`MyVratScreen`) — the personal ledger: shared cream metric band (`Saved vrats · Reminders on · This month`, Inter22 `saffronDeep` values), a Vrat reminder timings row, and the **My priority** list in saved order using the same illustrated calendar rows. Each row has the save/reminder bell and a separate gear for timing. A followed-muhurat section appears when applicable. Empty state uses the existing muted Karwa ritual painting, explains the bell and offers Browse Vrat & Parv. An inline OS settings notice explains undeliverable enabled reminders without changing Daily Bhakti settings.

**Vrat Reminder Sheet** (`VratReminderSheet.tsx`) — shared native transparent `Modal` over `modal-backdrop`, so nested scrolling catalog callers cannot position it off-screen. The body scrolls within 90% of screen height and has an explicit 44pt Close target. Grab handle (40×5, `divider`), advance-notice pills (`Off / 1 / 2 / 3 days`, evening), day-of Switch and time pills (`07:00 / 08:00 / Sunrise`, the existing labelled 06:00 proxy). Selected pills fill `saffron`; **Save reminders** commits, Close/backdrop cancels. Muhurat-specific options continue to reuse this component.

**Bell saves and Vrat preference reuse (28 October).** `useVratReminderAction` is shared by landing/search, category lists and detail. First use with no explicit saved Vrat preference opens the sheet; Save stores the Vrat default and adds the favourite, while Cancel changes neither. An explicit global default or prior per-Vrat choice is reused without setup, including a deliberately Off choice. Factory values and Daily Bhakti preferences never count as prior setup. `setReminder` also saves an unsaved Vrat. A second bell tap removes the saved Vrat and its reminders; My Vrat's separate gear edits per-Vrat timing. Preferences live in `VratFollowContext`: `@vedansh/vrat-follows`, `@vedansh/vrat-reminder-default` and `@vedansh/vrat-reminder-last` (retains a previous custom choice after unfollow). The built-in suggested timing remains 1-day advance +07:00. Daily Bhakti enablement/times remain separate. Only the shared OS permission is consulted: an explicit enabled reminder action may request an undetermined grant once; an OS denial is not reprompted. My Vrat shows an inline settings notice when enabled saved reminders cannot be delivered. Native evidence and limitations: `docs/evaluations/vrat-reminder-action-2026-10-27/`.

**Katha Library** (`KathaLibraryScreen`) — searchable list of every bundled bilingual katha: ॥ glyph, title, `<n> sections` caption, `पढ़ें · Read` affordance.

**Vrat Katha Reader** (`VratKathaReaderScreen` + `KathaSectionPage`) — lives in the **Home stack** (`VratKathaReader` route; Panchang surfaces navigate cross-tab to it) and is currently the **only route in `IMMERSIVE_HOME_ROUTES`** (`TabNavigator.tsx`), so the bottom tab bar hides for immersive reading. Plain `parchment` (no sketch). Top bar: **`ReaderHeader`** (§9) — 44 px back circle · katha title · `n / m` counter; then `ReadingProgressBar` and the Language Toggle (§16). Until July 2026 this screen was the most-drifted top bar in the app: a 40 px button, a 16 gutter and a hard-coded 18 title, all of which the shared header replaced. Body: a horizontal paged `FlatList` of section cards — each page carries a `प्रसंग · n/m` / `Part · n/m` pill (`versePill` tokens on `saffron-tint`), section title at 20 pt, a vertically-compressed `॥` Ornament, and body paragraphs at the shared `meaning` token (14 pt paragraph gap); long sections scroll vertically inside the page. §5 pager dots overlay the bottom; light haptic per page. The header carries the §39 **share circle at the right, after the counter** (`Share katha`) → a prose share with scopes *this part* / *whole katha* (§39.4–§39.6).

**Location Picker** (`LocationPickerModal.tsx`) — a `pageSheet` modal on plain `parchment`: title `स्थान चुनें · Choose location` + ✕; a "📍 Use my location" row (GPS fixes **snap to the nearest pincode centroid, or the nearest bundled city when that is closer** — see the tier table — with denied/error fallback copy); a search field taking **a city, town or village name, or a 6-digit pincode** (placeholder `शहर, गाँव या पिनकोड खोजें… · Search city, village or pincode…`); and the location list with a `saffron-deep` ✓ on the selection. Location state (`PanchangLocationContext`, `@vedansh:panchang-location`, default **Ujjain**) is the single reference for every location-sensitive computation; changing location warms that location's observance cache after interactions settle.

The list is **two browsable tiers**, rendered as one `FlatList` under group headers (12 pt, `ink-muted`, `letterSpacing` 0.4, 14/4 pt padding, script title face), plus **two search-only tiers** — places by name and pincodes by number:

| Group header | Source | Count |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `प्रमुख शहर · Major cities` | `MAJOR_CITIES` (`panchang/locations.ts`) | 52, Ujjain first (it is `DEFAULT_LOCATION`) |
| `राजस्थान · तहसील` / `Rajasthan · tehsils` | `RAJASTHAN_TEHSILS` (`panchang/rajasthanTehsils.ts`) | 342 across all 33 revenue districts |
| `कस्बे व गाँव` / `Towns & villages` | `pincodeOffices.json` via `searchPlaces` (`panchang/pincodes.ts`) | 165,192 India Post offices (810 head, 24,623 sub = towns, 139,759 branch = villages) — **search-only**, a name query of 3+ Latin letters, top 50 |
| `पिनकोड · Pincode` | `pincodeData.json` via `lookupPincode` | 19,654 nationwide — **search-only**, an exact 6-digit query |

- **Neither postal tier is a browse list.** Nobody scrolls 165k rows, and — decisively — neither source dataset carries Devanagari place names, so these entries cannot satisfy the `nameHi` contract every `City` row has (`utils/transliterate.ts` cannot go Latin → Devanagari, and machine-guessed spellings would be worse than a script mismatch).
- **Name query** (3+ characters with a Latin letter): the two city tiers filter as before, then `Towns & villages` lists matching post offices **below** them. Names and query are compared through a **spelling fold** (`foldPlaceName`: chh/ch → c, the h of bh/dh/gh/jh/kh/th/sh dropped, ph → f, w → v, z → j, doubled letters collapsed), so "Chhapra" finds India Post's "Chapra" and "Muzzafarpur" finds Muzaffarpur; when two names fold alike, the one spelled exactly as typed leads. A **district name** finds that district's head offices first ("Saran" → Chapra 841301; a district *prefix* only from four letters). Ranked: district heads and names starting with the query → a later word does → any substring; within each, head/sub offices (towns) before branch offices (villages), then the exact name, then shorter. So "srinagar" leads with Srinagar GPO, not the six villages called Srinagar. A town office within 25 km of a same-named listed city is dropped (Pokaran S.O beside the Pokaran tehsil) — the bilingual city row is the better answer. 1–2 characters search only the city tiers.
- **Place row**: title line `<Office> · <district>` in Latin at 15 pt (`numberOfLines` 1) in every language; caption is the Hindi — `५०४२७३ · तेलंगाना` (Devanagari digits + state; `504273 · Telangana` in English), 11 pt `ink-muted`. Each line is single-script (§3.0). Selecting one calls `selectPincode(pincode, name)`: **a place is its pincode** — same `pin-` id and the pincode's coordinate (office coordinates are the noisy half of the source) — and only `labelEn` remembers the name: `Kothimir · 504273, Telangana`. `labelHi` stays `५०४२७३ · तेलंगाना`. ✓ shows on the row whose pincode is selected **and** whose name heads the label.
- **Pincode query** (exactly 6 digits) replaces the whole list with a single resolved row; a well-formed but unknown code renders an explanatory line rather than an empty list.
- **Pincode row**: title line is `416001 · Maharashtra` (Devanagari digits and state name in Hindi/gu/kn — `४१६००१ · महाराष्ट्र`), caption is `<district> · <first three offices it serves> +N` in Latin — `Kamrup Metro · Guwahati GPO, Fancy Bazar, Uzanbazar` — because the district alone is often not the name a user knows. Selecting one stores `cityId: pin-416001`, `labelEn` `416001 · Kolhapur, Maharashtra`.
- **No match** (a name that matches no tier): an explanatory line, 12 pt `ink-muted`, never an empty sheet — "No place found — check the spelling, try a nearby town, or type the pincode." A **Devanagari** query with no city match is told the directory is Latin: "Search towns and villages in English letters (e.g. “Shahupuri”), or type the pincode." The hint under the field says the same up front (`कोई भी शहर, कस्बा, गाँव (अंग्रेज़ी अक्षरों में) या पिनकोड लिखें…`). GPS denied/error copy points at the search ("search for your town or village below").
- **Every stored `labelEn` ends in `, <State>`** spelled with "and" (`Jammu and Kashmir`, never `&`) — `lensRegistry.lensesForStoredLabel` reads the state off that tail.
- **GPS picks whichever tier is genuinely closer**, never "pincode always". Under the first geocoder a Srinagar fix found its nearest pincode 156 km away in Doda while the bundled Srinagar entry sat on top of it; coverage is complete now (all 186 J&K and 13 Ladakh pincodes) but the rule stands. Ties go to the city, which is the better label.
- **Both tables are lazily required** and never touch the launch path (`launchGraph.test.ts` forbids both): they load after interactions settle once this sheet opens — 605 KB of pincodes, then 3.4 MB of office names (display + precomputed folded copy), searched with native `indexOf` over the folded string (~1–4 ms a query in Node; ~50 ms one-time index build). `panchangPrefs` (which *is* the launch path, see §60) therefore validates a stored `pin-` record structurally instead of looking it up: a `pin-` id skips the bundled-city rebuild and is range-checked field by field, so the record is self-describing on disk.
- Data provenance, the coordinate gates and their measured accuracy live in `scripts/build-pincodes.mjs` (median 3.3 km against GeoNames points; was 5.4 km).

- **A tehsil is a `City` carrying `districtHi`/`districtEn`**; the national tier leaves both undefined, and the picker partitions the filtered list on that field rather than on an index. Groups whose filtered result is empty drop their header.
- **Row**: title line is the name in the current language + `· <district>` at 12 pt `ink-muted` (`numberOfLines` 1, `flexShrink`). Both halves are the same language, so one script face covers them — per §3.0 never mix a Devanagari district into a Latin caption. The caption line below stays the single-script cross-reference it always was.
- **Search** (`cityMatchesQuery`, exported from `locations.ts` and shared with the Kundali birth-city sheet) matches English name, Hindi name, and district in either script, so `alwar` surfaces all 21 Alwar tehsils.
- **The location chip (§ above) keeps showing the town alone**, not `town · district` — 12 pt in a pill has no room, and the picker's ✓ already resolves which same-named tehsil is active.
- **Birth-city sheet** (`KundaliScreen.tsx`) shares the same list, search predicate and `· district` caption, and is a `FlatList` rather than a mapped `ScrollView` — at ~390 rows, mounting every row stalled the sheet open.

---


### §33a Share card: पूर्ण पंचांग (`PanchangShareCard`)

**Purpose.** One image that carries the whole day the Panchang tab shows, sized for WhatsApp/feeds (4:5), branded, with the download link — so a forwarded panchang brings people back to the app.

**Entry points.** The sky header's share circle (§33 item 0, the selected date) and the Muhurat Detail top bar (that screen's date). Both call `usePanchangShare()` (`utils/panchangShare.tsx`) with the day's solve, its `MuhuratDay`, the date's observances, the location label and the calendar system; the sky side reads the muhurat with `useMuhurat(…, { live: false })`, so the screen gains no minute tick.

**Structure (540 × 675 dp, captured at 1080 × 1350):**
1. **Day sky** (144 dp, 22 pt bottom corners, `skyDay*` gradient): brand row — `splash-icon.png` 32 pt (radius 8) + approved compact `VedanshWordmark` over a separate `पूर्ण पंचांग` / `Full Panchang` subtitle (11.5/19 script serif or 12/18 Cormorant italic), with a 3dp gap — and the **place chip** (`dayGlass`, pin in `saffron`, the location label alone: a pincode label runs to `३९६२१० · दादरा और नगर हवेली और दमन और दीव`); the centred date (`सोमवार, 26 अक्टूबर 2026`, 22 / Cormorant 700 25 in English) and identity line (`<masa> <paksha> <tithi> · पूर्णिमांत · विक्रम संवत् 2083` — the month convention sits beside the masa it names); `ब्रह्म मुहूर्त` + `सूर्योदय` left and `सूर्यास्त` + `चंद्रोदय` right (13 + 14 clocks); a dashed sunrise→sunset arc with the sun between them.

**Share chrome alignment (3 November 2026).** Subtitle, location and Download CTA reserve full natural/script-safe line boxes rather than reader headroom's negative margin. The location keeps one-line auto-fit without fixed leading; its pin/label are centered in the glass chip. The Download label and Inter URL occupy separate centered boxes with a 6dp gap. Local per-face em corrections center the visible ink despite Indic descender reserve; they do not alter shared typography or reader headroom. Fixed output remains 540×675dp / 1080×1350px. Exact shipped-font metrics and browser/native evidence limits: `docs/reviews/2026-11-03-panchang-share-alignment.md`.
2. **पर्व ribbon** (only on observance days): 36 dp `cardActiveFrom` pill, moon mark on `skyNightTop`, at 15 — a lone observance as `आज · <name> · <deity>`, or when the day carries two or more, `आज · <name> · <name> · …` (every observance named, joined by `·`, no deity, no `+N`); the single line shrinks to fit.
3. **Angas** (`cardActiveFrom → parchmentGradientEnd`): तिथि · नक्षत्र at 19 over नित्य योग · करण at 16 (वार is already in the date line), each with `09:41 तक, फिर प्रतिपदा`. A क्षय day adds the second line (`06:20 तक, फिर चतुर्दशी (क्षय)` · `04:10, 1 अक्टू तक, फिर अमावस्या`). Successors: tithi/nakshatra/yoga by index; karana by `karanaSuccessorIndex` (movable cycle, fixed tail, विष्टि → शकुनि only in कृष्ण चतुर्दशी).
4. **All 16 चौघड़िया** (`parchmentSoft`): `दिन` / `रात` rows of eight `choghadiyaShubh` / `choghadiyaAvoid` bars, each with its name (13) and **start time** (13), plus the शुभ/त्याज्य legend.
5. **Kaal tiles** ×4 (`goldTint` अभिजीत, `avoidTint` राहु/गुलिक/यमगण्ड): label + one-line 24-hour window at 16 (`07:39 – 09:07`).
6. **वास tiles** ×4, widths weighted 0.75 / 1.05 / 0.9 / 1.3 (सूर्य · चन्द्रमा·चन्द्र वास · दिशा शूल · अग्नि वास): value at 15 + a line of up to two rows; दिशा शूल's value and a barred-havan line in `avoid`. अग्नि वास carries the only line that can run long (`हवन वर्जित · 02:04, 1 अक्टू से पाताल`), hence the widest tile.
7. **Footer** (50 dp, `skyNightUpper → skyNightTop`): the brand mark 36 pt in a `nightGold` ring, `रोज़ का पूरा पंचांग — वेदांश़ ऐप` / `मुहूर्त · चौघड़िया · व्रत-त्योहार · कुंडली · पाठ` (or the sharer's `<title> · <contact>`, *Co-branding* below), and a `nightGold` pill `डाउनलोड vedansh.app/get` — one Text with the link as an Inter span, so the script word and the link share a baseline.

**Co-branding (4 November 2026).** A pandit, temple or astrologer can sign the card as its publisher; Vedansh stays the download route. Opt-in profile (`data/shareBrand.ts`, store `utils/shareBrandStore.ts`, key `@vedansh/share-brand/v1`): `on`, `name` (≤28), `title` (≤28), `contact` (≤20). What the card prints (`resolveShareBrand`):
- **Off, or no name** → the plain card above, unchanged (no blank line).
- **Name, with or without title / contact** → footer line 2 (`panchang-share-brand-line`) becomes `<name>`, `<name> · <title>`, `<name> · <contact>` or `<name> · <title> · <contact>` (`shareBrandTextLine`) in place of `मुहूर्त · चौघड़िया · …`: `nightGold`, script title face at 12 / Cormorant 700 at 13, one line, shrinking to fit (min 0.75). Measured with the bundled fonts in the ≈278–293 dp text column beside the pill: a realistic profile (`पं. रमेश शर्मा · ज्योतिषाचार्य · 98290 12345`, 183 dp) prints at full size; the 28 + 28 + 20 maximum needs 0.83 in Devanagari, ≈0.73 in Gujarati/Kannada and 0.60 in Latin, so only those extreme profiles end in `…` (the share sheet's live preview shows it). A title or contact without a name is ignored.
- **The header is never touched** (revised 4 November 2026): no name chip; the brand row and place chip are exactly the plain card's. The first version (#612) put the name in a chip beside the city; it moved to the footer line because the co-branding agreed on the canvas was the footer slot (option A).
- The line is drawn in the face of the script it was typed in (`brandTextLang` over the whole line), not the card language, and never gains an automatic honorific. The Vedansh icon, wordmark, `रोज़ का पूरा पंचांग` line and the `डाउनलोड vedansh.app/get` pill never change.
- **Where it is set:** the share sheet's **मेरी ब्रांडिंग** block (`components/ShareBrandControls.tsx`, through `ShareableCard.controls`, §39.10) under the preview: title + sub-line + a `Switch` (off by default). Switching on with no name opens the editor instead; the editor has नाम (ज़रूरी), उपाधि (वैकल्पिक), संपर्क (वैकल्पिक) form fields, a one-line notice that the contact shows on every forwarded card and never enters the caption or hashtags, and रद्द करें / सहेजें. Saving with a name switches branding on; saving an empty name switches it off. Once saved, a summary row (name, line, edit icon) reopens the editor. A first-time profile starts from the Suvichar sender name, switched off. Everything is disabled while a capture runs.
- **Delivery.** The preview and every capture mount read the same saved profile (the card waits for it before reporting ready); `usePanchangShare` reads it before opening the sheet. Send as text ends with `<name> · <title> · <contact>`; the caption and hashtags never carry it. Sender photo: not in this version (the picker needs a store build).

**Type rules.** Every Text sets `allowFontScaling={false}`: the card is a fixed 540 × 675 image, and at a sharer's ~1.3× system text size its lines grew past the frame — the date line ran into ब्रह्म मुहूर्त and the वास row slid under the footer (Oct 2026). Every clock uses Noto Serif's lining digits (Cormorant's old-style figures read `11:40` as `II:40` at phone size); since that face is Noto Devanagari, the चौघड़िया start times and kaal windows take `withMarkHeadroom` in every language, English included, or their tops clip on device. Indic lines set tight leading, so each takes `withMarkHeadroom` (`utils/markHeadroom.ts`) or iOS/Android clip their matras. Single-line values use `adjustsFontSizeToFit` (min 0.85; the place chip 0.8) only as a last resort. Fit pass (rn-web, Oct 2026): real day + क्षय day + after-midnight ends + no-festival, with the widest names, the longest observance and the longest pincode label, in hi/en/gu/kn — hi, gu and kn fit at nominal size; English's three widest worst-case strings (place, festival, a क्षय line) shrink to 0.93–0.96. The body spaces its sections evenly (`space-between`); the tightest case (क्षय tithi + क्षय nakshatra + festival) leaves 3–5 dp.

**Delivery.** `usePanchangShare` hands the card to the shared provider as a self-branded `ShareableCard` (`panchangShareable`, §39.10), so it gets the Daily Bhakti picker: WhatsApp/other apps (1080×1350 with the message), Instagram post (already 4:5, scale 1) and story/reel (the 9:16 frame with `storyInset` — the card's text sits 12–14 dp from its edges, under the Reel's button column at full width, so it is placed at ≈0.81 with 52 dp side margins and rounded corners, clear of the top, bottom and right-column chrome; §39.3), card-language chips (the model is rebuilt per language) and Send as text (`buildPanchangShareText`). iOS: `Share.share({ message, url })`. Android: `shareFiles` (react-native-share) with the message; an older binary without it falls back to `expo-sharing`, image only — the link is still on the footer. The message is `पूर्ण पंचांग · <date> · <place>` + `रोज़ का पूरा पंचांग — Vedansh ऐप: https://vedansh.app/get` (`buildPanchangShareMessage`). The provider mounts the card off-screen only while a share runs and waits for the brand mark (at most 1.5 s); a second tap while the picker is open is ignored.

**Files.** `components/PanchangShareCard.tsx`, `panchang/panchangShareModel.ts` (pure; tested against the 26 Oct 2026 Bengaluru solve), `utils/panchangShare.tsx` (the provider card + hook), `panchang/panchangShareText.ts`, `data/shareLinks.ts`, `utils/shareVerse.tsx`; co-branding: `data/shareBrand.ts`, `utils/shareBrandStore.ts`, `components/ShareBrandControls.tsx`.

## 34. Audio Tab (भजन) & Now Playing

**Purpose.** A small devotional audio library — recitations of existing texts plus standalone bhajans/aartis — with playback that persists across the whole app. The tab (`AudioTab` → `AudioStackNavigator`) holds a single `AudioLibrary` screen; the mini-player and the full Now Playing surface are **root overlays** mounted once in `App.tsx`, driven by `AudioPlayerContext`, not navigation screens.

**Data.** `data/audio/tracks.ts` is a pure catalog (`AudioTrack`: bilingual title, thumb grapheme, deity, `kind: 'recitation' | 'standalone'`, `linkedTextId`, nominal duration). Audio bytes resolve separately via `assets/audio-library/index.ts` — a track surfaces **only** when `hasRealAudio(id)` is true (driven by `audioAssetManifest.json`), so nothing appears without a recording behind it. [13 tracks defined, 10 recordings today (`gayatri-mantra`, `hare-rama`, `govinda-hari-govinda`, `har-har-bhole`, `mahamrityunjay-mantra`, plus the standalone bhajans `govind-bolo`, `om-gam-ganapataye-namah`, `narayan-hari-hari`, `jai-nandlal-ki`, `krishnaya-vasudevaya`); the rest of the catalog is a labelled prototype Phase 2 curates. Each take is 128 kbps 48 kHz stereo MP3 with no embedded cover art (≈21 MB total).] **Delivery (R2 CDN):** the recordings are served from R2 and play **streaming-first** — `audioRemoteRequest(id)` gives the track's CDN url + cache key; `AudioPlayerContext` hands the player a local file when one is already cached (`assetCache.cachedFileUri`), otherwise the **CDN url directly** so it buffers and starts without waiting for a full download, and kicks a once-only background download (`cachedAssetUri`) into the OS-safe document dir. Every later play is the local file: instant and offline. The cache is a plain file — **no at-rest encryption** (the audio is already public on the CDN), so a tapped track starts fast every time, including a replay in a new session. `isBuffering` drives the play-control spinner during the brief initial buffer. Only `hare-rama.mp3` + `gayatri-mantra.mp3` stay bundled — the japam player `require`s them for instant offline alarm playback (§42-japam) — so ≈16 MB leaves the binary.

**Library screen** (`screens/audio/AudioLibraryScreen.tsx`), over the Home gradient:

1. Centred screen title `भजन` (reader-title face at 22, language-aware).
2. **Deity filter rail** — horizontal row of circular chips (§20 family): 54 px disc on the `cardThumbActiveFrom → cardThumbActiveTo` gradient with a `parchment-soft` Devanagari glyph, 11 pt label below; the selected chip gains a 2 px `saffron` ring on a `saffron-tint` pad. A leading `ॐ · सभी/All` chip clears the filter. Only deities that actually have an available track appear.
3. Sections with bilingual headings (`जारी रखें · Continue listening` when a track is loaded, `पाठ · Recitations`, `भजन व आरती · Bhajans & Aartis`), each a stack of `TrackCard`s.

**Track card** (`components/audio/TrackCard.tsx`) — the regular `ListCard` (§8) on an audio row: 52pt deity-icon thumb; bilingual title via `orderTitlesByLanguage` (titles wrap, never clamped); meta `पाठ · 8:14`-style. The tail swaps the navigate chevron for a 38 px **play disc**: `saffron-tint` + `saffron-deep` ▶ at rest, filled `saffron` + `on-primary` ❚❚ for the currently playing track. Tapping a card plays the track and opens Now Playing.

**Playback state** (`AudioPlayerContext`) — one imperative `expo-audio` player for the whole session (unlike the component-scoped `JapamAudioPlayer`), so playback survives navigation. The session is configured for background audio via `audio/audioSession.ts`: `playsInSilentMode`, with the interruption mode branched per platform in the **single `interruptionMode` field** — `mixWithOthers` on iOS, `duckOthers` on Android. Android must NOT rely on `interruptionModeAndroid` (expo-audio resolves `interruptionMode ?? interruptionModeAndroid`, so the iOS value overrides it) and must not use `mixWithOthers` (expo-audio then never requests audio focus, and Android 12+ force-mutes focus-less players when another app holds focus — the "silent playback on Android 16 devices" bug). Exposes position/duration, ±15 s skip (`SKIP_SECONDS`), next/previous across the playable set, loop, rate 0.5–1.5×, and `nowPlayingOpen`.

**Auto-advance.** A finished track rolls straight on to the next one in the playable set — the library plays through without a tap, and the loop/repeat toggle is what opts out of it (repeat on = the native `loop` flag restarts the same track, so no advance). The ending is detected from `didJustFinish` **or** a reported position within `END_EPSILON_SEC` (0.35 s) of the duration while stopped, because `didJustFinish` isn't emitted uniformly across platforms (the same platform caveat that pushed the japam bead counter to wall-clock timing, §35); a latch fires the advance exactly once per ending and re-arms as soon as playback moves off the end (resume, seek back, or a new source). Unlike the manual ◀◀/▶▶ buttons, auto-advance does **not** wrap: at the end of the library playback stops rather than cycling the catalog indefinitely in the background.
