**MiniPlayer** (`components/audio/MiniPlayer.tsx`) — rendered once at the app root; appears whenever a track is loaded and floats over every tab/stack. Docks just above the tab bar (bottom = 60 + safe-area inset + `spacing.xs`; inset `spacing.lg` each side) — mirroring the RoutineBanner's docking (§30). Card: `parchment-soft`, `divider` border, `radii.lg`, upward shadow; 40 px deity thumb on the thumb gradient; title (reader-title face at 15) over a 3 px progress strip (`saffron` fill on `divider` track); then ▶/❚❚ (`saffron-deep`) and ✕ (stop & dismiss) buttons at 36 px. Tapping the body expands Now Playing.

**Now Playing** (`screens/audio/NowPlayingScreen.tsx`) — a full-screen `parchment` overlay (absolute-fill, mounted app-wide in `App.tsx`; no navigation plumbing), shown when `nowPlayingOpen`:

- Header: ⌄ minimise circle · uppercase `Now Playing` label (swipe-hint face) · spacer.
- Artwork: a 220 px `parchment-soft` framed square with the deity art at 150 (the painted `deity` seal when a track has no deity).
- Title at 26 centred (reader-title face), subtitle `<artist/kind> · <deity>` in Cormorant italic `ink-muted`.
- Seek bar: 4 px `saffron` fill on `divider` track, tap-to-seek; lining time labels either side (Cormorant SemiBold 15).
- Transport row: `−15 · ◀◀ · [▶/❚❚] · ▶▶ · +15`; the play button is a 72 px `saffron` disc with `saffron-deep` rim and `on-primary` glyph.
- Secondary row: a 44 px ⟳ loop toggle (kept for mantra japa) — outline at rest, filled `saffron` when looping. It doubles as the **repeat-one** control: while it is on, a finished track restarts instead of auto-advancing to the next one.

**Reader entry point.** Readers whose text has a linked recitation with real audio (via `getTrackForText` + `hasRealAudio`) show a small `saffron-deep` **▶** in the top bar after the page counter (`ChalisaReaderScreen.tsx`); tapping plays the recitation and opens Now Playing — the structural "audio hook" §9 reserved. Since July 2026 that `▶` is joined by the **read-aloud** control (§56), which speaks the text with the device voice for the many sections that have no recording. The two are **mutually exclusive**: both claim playback through `src/audio/playbackArbiter.ts`, so starting one silences the other — load-bearing on iOS, whose session is configured `mixWithOthers` and would otherwise play both at once. Read-aloud deliberately has **no mini-player and no lock-screen surface**: `expo-speech` exposes no media-session API, so it stays reader-scoped and stops when the app backgrounds.

---

## 35. Japam (जप)

**Purpose.** A mantra-counting practice surface: tap (or let the audio loop count for you) through 108-bead rounds of a chosen mantra. Mantras are first-class library entries (`category: 'japam'` in `texts.ts`, sourced from `data/japam/japam.json` — 22 today: ॐ नमः शिवाय, हरे कृष्ण महामंत्र, गायत्री मंत्र, ॐ नमो भगवते वासुदेवाय, the **राधा नाम जप** naam japa tagged to Radha Rani, the **जय श्री राम जप** naam japa tagged to Shri Rama, सरस्वती मूल मंत्र, महामृत्युंजय मंत्र (Rigveda 7.59.12, tagged Shiva, on the Shiva sketch; the same text as Shiva Stotram ch. 3, whose जप pill opens this entry — §35.1), ॐ गं गणपतये नमः, ॐ नमो नारायणाय, हनुमान मूल मंत्र (ॐ श्री हनुमते नमः), श्री राम जय राम जय जय राम, दुर्गा बीज मंत्र (ॐ दुं दुर्गायै नमः), and the **nine Navagraha beej mantras** (सूर्य … केतु; tagged `navagraha`, plus `surya`/`shani` for theirs; sub `नवग्रह बीज मंत्र · <vaar>`; on the Navagraha plate, Surya and Shani on their own; card art follows the first tag — the Navagraha nine-disc seal for Chandra…Ketu, the rising sun for Surya, the ringed graha for Shani, and each deity mantra its deity's attribute — so none shows its text `thumb`). A beej entry's line is its `GRAHA_UPAY` mantra word for word (`GRAHA_BEEJ_JAPA_ID`, `data/japam/grahaBeej.ts`; `grahaBeejJapa.test.ts` pins it) and its meaning ends `a practice for steadiness, not a fix or a guarantee` (RULEBOOK §14.3.5); the Kundali upay panel (§78) and the Dasha tab open these counters; like naam lines they are left out of the daily-verse pool (`versePool.ts`) — upaya stays on the surfaces the person opened, never pushed as a verse of the day), so they surface through the normal catalog/deity/search flows; `buildEntryStartTarget` routes a japam entry straight to `JapamCounter` (Home stack, lazy-loaded).

**Counter screen** (`JapamCounterScreen.tsx`). Layer stack: parchment · per-mantra sketch (`getSourceBackground`) · content.

**One screen, no scroll** (Oct 2026 redesign): everything — mantra, ring, count, today's tally, audio — fits one viewport above the visible tab bar on a 6.1" phone, and a 4-line mantra still fits an iPhone SE. The language pill moved into the top bar, the hint moved inside the ring, the tray + today line became one fixed stats row, and the two reset buttons became one icon.

1. **Top bar** (8 gap): 44 px back circle · the screen name `जपमाला / JapaMala / જપમાળા / ಜಪಮಾಲೆ` (the mantra itself is shown once, in the tap surface — short mantras used to appear twice) · the **compact** Language Toggle (§16, `हिं / En` short labels) · a 34 px ⏰ alarm button (opens the shared `AlarmEditorSheet` pre-locked to this mantra) · Share button.
2. **Tap surface** — the whole remaining column is one large Pressable (inside a ScrollView so a large-type mantra scrolls rather than clips; pressed state dims to 0.92):
   - Mantra lines, centred, at the shared `verse` token (23/39; Latin languages use Cormorant italic scaled by the reading-size factor, §44).
   - `॥` Ornament, **compact** (6 px vertical margins instead of 26).
   - **Turning mala** (`components/JapamMala.tsx`, pure geometry in `components/japamMalaGeometry.ts`) replaces the old big numeral + progress track. 108 wooden beads on a gold thread with a gap at the bottom for the brass Sumeru + tassel; a fixed `saffron` marker sits at the top. Each bead turns the ring (Animated rotate, 260 ms ease-out) so the bead under the fingers is always under the marker: it is brass with a `saffron-deep` stroke, chanted beads darken, beads ahead stay light wood. Every completed round **flips the direction** (`malaDirection(rounds)`) — the Sumeru is never crossed, the mala is turned — and a `सुमेरु · माला पलटें` notice (`saffron-deep`) replaces the hint under the count for 2.2 s. The centre holds a faint `gold` ॐ watermark (40 % of the box, 0.14 opacity) behind the count — the bead number in Cormorant SemiBold 46 (`ink`) beside a small `/ 108` (Inter 13, `ink-muted`) — and under it the resting hint (12, `ink-muted`, up to 2 lines within the ring): `जप के लिए स्पर्श करें · Tap to chant`, or while the audio loop plays `सुनें · मंत्र के साथ मनके आगे बढ़ते हैं / Listening · beads advance with the chant` with the top marker pulsing (0.35↔1 opacity, 1.4 s). All motion drops to instant under reduced motion. Box size is **fitted to the tap surface**: the ScrollView viewport minus the measured head (mantra + ornament), clamped to 230–330 and capped by width (below 230 the 108 beads overlap, so the surface scrolls instead). The ring is held at opacity 0 until that first measurement so it never visibly snaps. The screen sits above the visible tab bar, so its `SafeAreaView` omits the `bottom` edge (the tab bar owns that inset — the double inset was the ~34 px dead band that pushed the ring past the audio row). Bead wood/brass colours are material constants, not theme roles. The ring is hidden from screen readers; the tap surface's label carries the count.
   - **Naam japa** (`naam: true` in `japam.json`; today `radha-naam`, line `राधा`, and `jai-shri-ram`, line `जय श्री राम`): the line is a single divine name (or a short naam jaikara), so it reads larger (1.5× the verse size, Latin 1.6×), the entry's name (`राधा नाम जप / Radha Naam Japa`) sits under it as a 13 `ink-muted` caption, and every bead chanted on the screen gives the name a small pulse (scale 1.14 → 1, 460 ms ease-out; none for the count restored on open, none under reduced motion). The hint reads `स्पर्श करें · हर नाम एक मनका / Tap · one bead per name` and the stats row counts `आज N नाम / N names today`. Naam lines are left out of the daily-verse pool (`versePool.ts`) — a single name is not a verse. Radha naam has no recording yet, so the audio row shows the "Audio not available" notice; when one is bundled, a single clean `राधा` of ≥ ~1 s looped (one bead per loop) keeps each bead on a spoken name exactly, whereas a kirtan rendition's `repetitions` estimate drifts.
   - Each tap = one bead with a light haptic; completing a round (bead 108 rolls the counter) fires a success haptic. Counts persist per mantra in `JapamCounterContext` (`@vedansh/japam-counter`). `JAPAM_BEADS_PER_ROUND = 108` (`data/japam/index.ts`).
3. **Stats row** (fixed, outside the scroll, ≥56 tall, above a `divider` hairline; nothing in it can change height, so the ring never jumps mid-japa):
   - **Completed malas** (`JapamMalaTray`): the completed-round count as a Cormorant Bold numeral (28, `saffron-deep`) beside `माला पूर्ण / Malas done` (11, `ink-muted`) with one mini mala (16 px dashed wooden ring + brass Sumeru) per completed round below the label — capped at 6, then a `+N` pill on `saffron-tint`. A round completed while the screen is open drops its mini in (spring). Zero rounds shows italic `पहली माला आरम्भ / First mala on its way` in the minis' place.
   - A 1 × 28 `divider` rule, then `आज N जप / N japa today` (reader-title face at 14, `saffron-deep`, one line) — today's beads for this mantra from `UserActivityContext`.
   - **↺ reset** — a 44 px `parchment-soft` circle with a `cardActiveBorder` hairline and the `reset` icon (`saffron-deep`); disabled at 0.4 when there is nothing to reset. It opens a **bottom sheet** over `modal-backdrop` (grip, `गिनती साफ़ करें? / Clear the count?`) with both choices as full-width option cards: `बीज पुनः ० · Reset Beads` (`parchment-soft` / `cardActiveBorder`, `saffron-deep` title; zeroes the current round, keeps completed rounds; disabled when the round is at 0) and `सब साफ़ · Clear All` (`avoid-tint` / `avoid` hairline, `avoid-deep` title; erases beads and rounds). Each card carries its consequence as an italic line; picking one acts at once — the sheet is the confirmation — and Cancel closes it.
4. **Audio row** (`components/JapamAudioPlayer.tsx`, above a `divider` hairline, one line — Play at the start, the Tempo stepper with its label **inline** at the end; reports play state to the screen via `onPlayingChange` for the listening hint/marker pulse, and taps still add beads while it plays): an auto-chant loop of the mantra recording (`assets/japam-audio`). A `▶ चलाएँ / Play` pill (fills `saffron` while playing) and a **Tempo** stepper (− / 1.0× / +, range 0.5–1.5× in 0.1 steps, pitch-corrected). The clip loops natively and the bead count advances with the chanting on a **wall-clock timer** (`components/japamBeadProgress.ts`, a pure reducer): a single-recitation clip registers **one bead per loop-length of play**, while a musical rendition declares how many times it chants the mantra (`repetitions` in `assets/japam-audio`) and registers **one bead per repetition segment** (one per `duration ÷ repetitions` of play time), so a multi-minute kirtan doesn't count as a single bead. Counting is timed, not driven by the reported playback position, because native looping doesn't reliably report a position wrap — on some devices the position climbs past the duration or stalls, which froze the old position-based counter after the first loop. The timer is gated on the player's own **play/pause intent** (`chanting` state), NOT the native `status.playing` flag: that flag flickers false at every loop seam and its update stream can stall, which both flickered the Play button and froze the count after the first loop. The Play/Pause label, the `onPlayingChange` listening-hint signal and the counter are all driven off this intent, so the button never flickers mid-loop; elapsed play time (scaled by the tempo, paused only on an explicit pause/stop, and ignoring long background gaps) keeps the beads moving for the whole session. `autoPlay` starts the loop immediately when arriving from an alarm tap (§38) and **auto-stops after 30 s** (`ALARM_AUTO_STOP_MS`) so the alarm rings the mantra from the shared recording then falls silent rather than looping forever — reusing the same bundled mp3, with no separate size-adding alarm clip; any manual play/pause cancels the cap so a real chanting session isn't cut off. Mantras without a bundled clip show an italic "Audio not available" notice instead. Bundled today: `om-namah-shivaya` (own single-recitation clip, 1 bead/loop) plus `hare-krishna-mahamantra` and `gayatri-mantra`, which reuse the Audio-library rendition takes (`assets/audio-library/hare-rama.mp3` ≈4 reps — the 2:14 mahamantra rendition, `gayatri-mantra.mp3` ≈6 reps) via a relative `require` so the mp3 is bundled once rather than duplicated. The per-file `repetitions` counts are cadence estimates — tune them if a round drifts from 108 beads.

**Mantra selection.** There is no dedicated picker screen — the japam category tile / deity lists / search / routine items open the counter for a specific mantra; the alarm editor's mantra picker (below) is the one in-flow chooser — plus every stotram reader's **जप** pill, which opens the counter on the shloka on screen (§35.1).

**Alarms** (`JapamAlarmsScreen.tsx` in the More stack, `JapamAlarmsContext`, cap `MAX_JAPAM_ALARMS = 8`, persisted at `@vedansh/japam-alarms`):

- List screen over the Home gradient: intro line ("Wake to the mantra you chose, at the time you chose."), a permission banner when notifications are denied (tap → system settings; "denied" is the **effective** status of §38 — never a fresh Android install that hasn't been asked yet), alarm rows (`parchment-soft`, `radii.lg`: the time in the reader-title face — always 24-hour `HH:MM` (§3.2), whatever the device locale — mantra name, a repeat line "`Daily / Once / Weekdays / Weekends / day list` · `in 7 hr 25 min`" (live 30 s tick) plus "skips ‹date›" while a skip is pending, optional uppercase label, and a `saffron` Switch), and an outline `+ Add alarm` button. (No privacy footnote — implementation-detail messaging like "nothing goes to the server" is deliberately not shown anywhere in the app.)
- **AlarmEditorSheet** (exported and reused by the counter's ⏰) in a fade modal, internally scrollable (`maxHeight` 88 %): `TimeStepper` at **1-minute** steps (a chevron steps once per tap on press-release; the auto-repeat starts from **long-press** — 350 ms, then every 90 ms — so a scroll drag that begins on a chevron never mutates the time), a **Repeat** row of seven 38 px circular day chips (S M T W T F S; all on = Daily, none = Once — the summary line then warns "turns off after ringing"), the mantra picker (locked when opened from a counter), a **Label** `TextInput` (40 chars, optional), an edit-mode-only **Skip next** toggle chip showing the date it would skip, and a live "Rings in …" preview line above the filled confirm button (a11y label `Confirm alarm`).
- **Model** (`notifications/japamAlarms.ts`): `repeatDays?: number[]` (getDay() indices; absent = daily, `[]` = one-time, subset = weekly) and `skipNextDate?: 'YYYY-MM-DD'`. Pure helpers: `nextAlarmFireTimestamp(s)` (honours days + skip), the shared skip plan (`isSkipPending` / `skipOneshotPlan` — one pendency predicate and `:occN` id scheme for every tier), `repeatSummary`, `describeUntilFire`, `formatTimeLabel` (always 24-hour `HH:MM`, §3.2). The context clears `skipNextDate` whenever time/repeat change, prunes past skip dates on load/foreground, and **auto-disables fired one-time alarms** via the scheduler's once-armed bookkeeping (`firedOnceAlarmIds`; merge semantics — a past armed timestamp is evidence the alarm rang and is never overwritten, and a fired one-time alarm is never re-armed).
- Scheduling (`notifications/japamAlarmScheduler.ts`) is tiered: Android uses the native module (`AlarmManager.setAlarmClock` + a high-importance lock-screen notification with the mantra sound on the **alarm stream** — `USAGE_ALARM` audio attributes, so it rings through vibrate/silent and follows the alarm volume slider like the Clock app, matching AlarmKit's override-silent semantics on iOS — and **Stop / Snooze 5 m** actions — survives Doze and reboot; the user-controlled `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` access is requested through the screen's explanatory **Alarms & reminders** banner, otherwise Android falls back to an inexact Doze-tolerant alarm; the app deliberately does not declare Play-restricted `USE_EXACT_ALARM` or `USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT`; the Kotlin receiver re-arms the next *repeat day* after each fire, never for one-shots, dismisses by the exact posted notification key, and suppresses a snooze fire whose base alarm is no longer armed); iOS 26+ uses AlarmKit (weekly recurrence on the selected days, `.fixed` one-shots for Once, native **Snooze** countdown button; reconcile leaves a mid-countdown alarm untouched so opening the app never swallows a snoozed re-ring; a bare `repeatDays: []` means one-shot on both platforms); older iOS / Expo Go falls back to `expo-notifications` — DAILY trigger for daily, one WEEKLY trigger per selected day, DATE one-shots for Once, all under a `JAPAM_EXPO_SLOT_CAP = 24` pending-slot budget (whole-alarm granularity, soonest-first) so japam can't crowd the daily-verse window out of iOS's 64-pending cap, plus a `japam-alarm` notification category carrying a **Snooze 5 min** action (`maybeHandleJapamSnoozeResponse`, wired in App.tsx on the **live listener only** — the cold-start "last response" is ignored so a stale snooze tap can't schedule a phantom ring). A pending skip-next on recurrence-owning tiers is armed as discrete one-shots — `ALARMKIT_SKIP_ONESHOT_COUNT = 7` / `EXPO_SKIP_ONESHOT_COUNT = 4` — and reverts to plain recurrence on the next foreground reconcile. `scheduleJapamAlarms` is idempotent and **serialized** (concurrent reconciles chain, last caller wins) — cancel-then-reschedule on any change; in-flight `:snooze` one-shots for live alarms are spared while orphaned ones (alarm deleted/disabled) are cancelled. Tapping the alarm deep-links into the counter with `autoPlay` (§38).
- **Ring tune** (`assets/japam-alarm-sounds/index.ts`): the alarm rings the chosen mantra's own bundled clip — a ≤30 s mono 22.05 kHz PCM WAV resolved by `getJapamAlarmSoundName(mantraId)` on every tier (AlarmKit `.named()`, the Android receiver's `res/raw` lookup by underscored mantra id, and a per-mantra expo notification channel, since Android 8+ pins a channel's sound at creation). Android channels use **`-v2`/`:v2` ids** on both tiers: the v1 channels rang on the notification stream (muted by vibrate/silent and the notification-volume slider — the "no alarm volume on Android" bug) and a pinned channel can't be re-attributed, so the alarm-stream fix ships as fresh channels while the v1 ones are deleted on ensure. The Kotlin receiver's channel sound is a **name-based** `android.resource://<pkg>/raw/<name>` URI, never the int resource id (raw ids renumber across app updates and silently kill a pinned sound). Bundled today: `om-namah-shivaya` (single recitation, ~5.6 s) plus `hare-krishna-mahamantra` and `gayatri-mantra` (28 s loudness-normalised, faded excerpts cut from the Audio-library takes). A mantra without a clip (every entry but those three — `om-namo-bhagavate-vasudevaya`, `radha-naam`, `jai-shri-ram`, `saraswati-moola-mantra`, `mahamrityunjaya-mantra`, the four deity mantras added with it and the nine graha beej mantras) falls back to the system tone. A new clip must **also** be listed in `app.json` → `expo-notifications.sounds[]` — without that it isn't copied into the native bundles and the alarm silently falls back to the default chime. The bundled clip **filenames use underscores** (`om_namah_shivaya.wav`), not the hyphenated mantra id: `expo-notifications` copies each file into Android `res/raw/` verbatim and rejects hyphens at prebuild, so a hyphenated filename fails the Android build. The receiver bridges the two with `mantraId.replace('-','_')`.


### 35.1 Sloka japa — any stotram shloka on the mala (Oct 2026)

**Purpose.** Any shloka of a स्तोत्रम्-category text can be chanted on the same 108-bead mala **exactly like a mantra**: one tap, one recitation of that shloka (shloka japa / samput). The shloka's lines and meaning are the ones the reader shows; nothing is authored twice. Ashtakam, Suktam, Kavacham, Chalisa and Aarti are deliberately out of scope. Chosen over an "advance one shloka per tap" variant after an interactive A/B prototype (Oct 2026): a japa surface holds one text still under the thumb.

**Entry.** `components/StartJapaButton.tsx` — a labelled pill on the reader's language-toggle row, pinned to the **left edge** (`japaSlot`: `position: absolute`, `left: 16`, `top: 6`, `bottom: 12`, `justifyContent: center`), mirroring the read-aloud pill on the right (§56.2) so the centred toggle + `＋` group keeps its room on a 375 pt phone. Same pill as read-aloud idle: `repeat` AppIcon (14) + `जप / Japa / જપ / ಜಪ` at 13, `saffron-deep` on `saffron-tint`, `cardActiveBorder` hairline, `radii.sm`, 10 hitSlop; a11y label `Start as japa` (English, Maestro-stable). On all nine stotram readers — Shiva / Durga / Saraswati / Ganesh / Krishna Stotram, Vishnu Sahasranama, Bajrang Baan, Ram Stuti and the `StutiReader` (Krishna Stuti, Durga Stuti, Kubera, Navagraha). The reader passes its current page (`verseIndex`); tap → `JapamCounter { mantraId: <sloka id> }` on the Home stack. On a chapter's title card (verse 0 of the `sanskrit`-shaped corpora) the pill starts that chapter's first shloka. A shloka that is also a list mantra (`LIST_MANTRA` in `sloka.ts`; today Shiva Stotram ch. 3 → `mahamrityunjaya-mantra`) opens that mantra instead, so the same chant keeps one count and one today tally wherever it is started; the sloka id still resolves for counts and alarms made before the mapping.

**One shloka = one sloka id** (`data/japam/sloka.ts`): `sloka.<sourceId>.<chapter>.<number>`, keyed by the verse's own `number` (stable across content edits, unlike its page index); single-chapter registry texts use chapter 1. Ids are URL-safe because the Japam widget deep link carries them. `findJapamMantra` resolves them, so counts (one per shloka), today's tally, alarms, the widget, notification taps and Profile all key a shloka exactly like a mantra; slokas never join `japamMantras` (library, search, Ask and the alarm picker list stay mantras-only). The entry's name is `<stotra> · <shloka label>` — `शिव ताण्डव स्तोत्रम् · श्लोक 5`, `बजरंग बाण · दोहा` (the verse's own `labelHi/En` when it has one), and `<section> · <part> · …` for a stotra spanning chapters (Vishnu Sahasranama).

**Counter for a shloka** (`mantra.sloka` set) — §35 unchanged except:
- **Head**: the shloka's lines at the `verse` token, with the entry's name under them as the 13 `ink-muted` caption naam japa uses, so the counter says which shloka it is.
- **Background**: the section's sketch (`getSourceBackground(sloka.sourceId)`).
- **Hint** `स्पर्श करें · हर श्लोक एक मनका / Tap · one bead per shloka`; **today** `आज N श्लोक / N shloka today`.
- **Audio row**: no recording exists, so it shows the existing "Audio not available" notice.
- **Share**: the shloka and its meaning. **Alarm** ⏰ works unchanged (the editor is locked to the shloka); editing such an alarm from the Alarms list shows it as a leading chip in the picker.

Tests: `data/__tests__/slokaJapa.test.ts` (registry ↔ stotram category, every shloka resolves, title-card rule, labels, URL-safe ids), `JapamCounterMala.test.tsx` (shloka head + bead), `.maestro/stotram-smoke.yaml` (Bajrang Baan → जप → one bead).

## 36. Search

**Purpose.** On-device search across the entire library — sections, deities, and every verse — built from the same bundled data the readers load. No network, no service, no query logging. The index (`data/searchIndex.ts`) is built **in the background after launch** — ~8 ms slices of one chapter or one temple each, alongside the screen warm-up (§75) — so it is normally ready before Search is ever opened. Opening Search never blocks on it (`screens/_useSearchIndex.ts`): if the build is still running, the screen renders at once and takes the same job over at a foreground pace.

**Entry point — floating button** (`components/SearchFloatingButton.tsx`). A 48 px circle, `parchment-soft` fill, 1 px `divider` border, holding a `saffron` ⌕ glyph at 26 (reader-title face). Anchored absolute at `right: spacing.xl`, `bottom: spacing.xl` (the default). It used to pass a banner-clearing offset on Home to sit above the docked RoutineBanner; since that banner moved inline (§30, July 2026) the FAB uses the default offset and keeps a positive `zIndex` so it stays above the scroll content. Currently rendered on Home only; tap → `Search` (Home stack).

**Search screen** (`SearchScreen.tsx`), over the Home gradient:

1. **Top bar**: 44 px back circle + a pill-shaped input row (`parchment-soft`, `divider`, `radii.md`, 44 high): `saffron` ⌕, the `TextInput` (Inter 500 at 15, language-aware placeholder "श्लोक, पाठ, मंत्र खोजें…"), and a ✕ clear button while typing. The input auto-focuses ~200 ms after mount.
2. **Empty state** (no query): *Recent* chips (last 6 queries, `@vedansh/search-recent`; pill chips with per-chip ✕ and a `Clear All` action) and a *Popular* 2-up grid of four fallback sections (Hanuman Chalisa, Gita, Sundarkand, Shiva Stotram) as thumb-glyph cells.
3. **Results** — grouped rows under `sectionLabel`-style headers with counts (`पाठ · Sections`, `देवता · Deities`, `श्लोक · Verses`):
   - *Section row*: Devanagari thumb glyph (`saffron-deep`), name in the active language, Hindi subtitle, `saffron` ›. Tap → the section's start via `navigateToEntryStart` (§38) — chalisa readers, chapters indexes, aarti/sanskar readers, the japam counter, or the Theerth map as appropriate. **Vidhi rows** (PRD-19 Phase 2B) ride this group too — one row per published vidhi (॥ thumb, `पूजा विधि · N चरण` subtitle); their sourceId is the vidhi id and tap opens `VidhiDetail` (§62) instead of a reader — pushed on the Home stack, which registers the vidhi flow alongside the Panchang one, so back returns to the search results.
   - *Deity row*: `gold` ॐ thumb; tap → `DeityList` filtered by that deity.
   - *Verse row*: the matched verse's first line in the verse face at 17/28, source · label meta in Cormorant italic at 13/20; section/deity row names are 16/26 over a 12/18 subtitle — every single-line row that can carry Devanagari keeps ≥ 1.5× leading so above-shirorekha matras (ि ी) are not sliced off (§3.0; the verse line shipped at 17/24 and clipped, September 2026); tap → **that verse in its reader** via `buildProgressTarget` (chapter + verse index), falling back to the section start.
   - Verse hits are capped at `VERSE_RESULT_CAP = 50`, with an italic "More results — type a more specific query" note when clipped.
4. **Zero state**: dimmed `॥`, "कोई परिणाम नहीं / No matches found", and a hint to try a Devanagari word or section name.
5. **Preparing state** — only when a query is typed before the index has finished building (a search within seconds of a cold launch): a `saffron` `ActivityIndicator` over "खोज तैयार हो रही है… / Preparing search…" in the zero state's `ink-muted` meaning face, in the zero state's slot. It must never read "no matches" — nothing has been searched yet — and the Ask abstain card likewise does not claim the library is empty while it shows. Announced as a polite live-region progress bar. Replaced by results (or the zero state) the moment the index lands.

**Index coverage.** Sections (every active library entry **plus one row per published vidhi** — vidhis are procedures, so they contribute no verse entries), deities, and verses from every text module — the twelve chalisas, aartis, japam mantras, Gita, Sundarkand, all stotram modules, Ramcharitmanas, Valmiki Ramayan, sanskar items, and the Theerth temples. Standard `lines`/`linesEn` shapes are picked up automatically when a section is added (RULEBOOK §7).

**Normalization** (`data/searchNormalize.ts`) — one pure fold applied to both index and query, so Devanagari and Latin queries meet in the middle: Unicode NFD with the combining nukta stripped (क़ ⇄ क), lowercase, IAST diacritics folded to ASCII (`kṛṣṇa` → `krsna`, so a plain-ASCII query matches the romanized corpus), punctuation dropped **including daṇḍa `।`/`॥`**, whitespace collapsed. Ranking is exact > prefix > substring per field (`MatchRank`), idempotent and unit-tested.

## 37. More Hub & Profile

**Purpose.** The settings-and-self hub, opened by Home’s top-right gear (`MoreTab` → `MoreStackNavigator`: `MoreHome` → `Profile` / `Wishlist` / `Reminders` / `JapamAlarms` / `WidgetGallery` / `PitruSmaran*` + `PitruPakshaOverview` / `JanmaTithi*` + `KulParampara*` (§70) / `VastuDisha` / the vidhi trio + `GitaReader`). One scroll over the Home gradient, **16 px gutters**, three grouped sections ~22 apart. **All hub chrome is single-language** (the selected reading language only) — bilingual pairing is reserved for actual reading content, never navigation/settings (V4 redesign).

**Hub** (`MoreScreen.tsx`), top to bottom:

1. **Title** — one left-aligned line, selected language only (`सेटिंग्स` / `Settings` / `સેટિંગ્સ` / `ಸೆಟ್ಟಿಂಗ್‌ಗಳು`), with a 44 pt back button returning to Home, 30 pt in the script's title face (`latinBold` for en, `scriptTitleFont` for hi/gu/kn). No `More` subtitle.
2. **Three grouped inset lists** — each is an uppercase **group label** (`saffron-deep`, 13; Latin gets tracking + uppercase via the chrome font, Indic drops both) above one **list container** (`parchment-soft`, **`radii.lg`**, 1 px `divider`, `overflow:hidden`, **`elevation.subtle`**) whose rows are split by hairline `divider` top-borders. Standard row anatomy: `[38dp fixed icon tile, 30dp painted art, radii.sm] [label 18]  …  [state 15 ink-muted] [17dp shared chevron, iconInk]`. All row artwork uses the More Storybook family, with centered transparent bounds and unchanged pigments. The container radius was an ad-hoc 20 and the icon tile 11, both off the radius scale (§4), with a hand-rolled shadow; all three are tokens as of July 2026, padding 15×16, pressed → `saffron-tint` wash.
   - **साधना / Practice** — a compact **profile hero row** (tinted `cardActiveFrom → cardActiveTo` gradient, 52dp circular `goldTint` badge with 32dp painted ॐ, centered in the same 38dp column as the settings rows, `साधक प्रोफ़ाइल` title, sub-line "**`N`** श्लोक · **`N`** श्रृंखला" = lifetime verses + streak in `saffron`; the old `rounds` count is dropped; a11y "Open Sadhak profile" → Profile), then the rows **in importance order** (Sept 2026) — three tiers, each ranked within itself: **(a) the daily-practice loop**, most-used first: **स्मरण** (painted brass bell, state = reminder time(s) or Off → Reminder Settings §38; the default-on habit driver), **जप अलार्म** (painted antique alarm clock, state = active count → §35), **संग्रह** (ochre bookmark with cream heart, state = saved count; label matches the WishlistScreen title → Wishlist §24); **(b) the family & lineage records**, by dharmic weight: **कुल परम्परा** (three painted family silhouettes, state = the saved kuldev's name, else NEW → §70; the household's foundational record), **पितृ स्मरण** (painted lotus, state = NEW while empty, then `count · soonest date` → §63; the recurring shraddha duty), **जन्म तिथि** (painted calendar/star, same NEW/`count · soonest` state pattern → §70; celebration); **(c) the occasional home tool**: **वास्तु दिशा** (painted compass, NEW state → §66). A new practice row slots into its tier by the same rule rather than appending at the bottom; `MoreScreen.test.tsx` pins the order.
   - **ऐप / App** — **भाषा** (painted A/अ translation tiles, state = current language's native name; opens the **Language picker sheet**, not an inline grid), **पाठ का आकार** (painted Aa, state = मानक/बड़ा; opens the **Reading-size picker sheet**, §43), **पाठ सुनें / Read Aloud** (painted brass speaker, state = what will be spoken + the rate via the exported `readAloudRowLabel`, or `उपलब्ध नहीं` when the device has no voice; opens the **Read-aloud settings sheet**, §56), **ऐप साझा करें**
     Both settings rows are also feature-tour spotlight targets (`languageRow` / `readingSizeRow`, §47 steps 23–24): each `SettingsRow` is wrapped in a measurable `View` and registers a `scrollNodeIntoView` reveal against the More `ScrollView`, since the App group can sit below the fold. The tour ends on them, and the post-tour setup sheet then asks the user to set both. (painted share nodes; OS share sheet via `buildAppShareMessage(lang)`, `data/shareLinks.ts` — the localized `APP_SHARE_INVITE` + `SMART_LINK`. The invite is a **multi-line feature list**, not a one-liner: a "complete bhakti in one app" lede, seven `•` bullets — texts (Gita/Ramayan/Sundarkand/Chalisa/Aarti/Stotra), illustrated kids' bhakti stories, vrat katha + puja vidhi + tirth guide, Panchang (vrat-festival/muhurat/kundali/rashifal), japa mala + alarms + bhajan audio + daily verse, nitya-sadhana routine, Ask Vedansh (जिज्ञासा: today's tithi/vrat/muhurat) — only features a fresh install can open, never store-gated or draft surfaces — a four-script "read in" language line, the download CTA with the smart link (`SMART_LINK` = `https://vedansh.app/get`), and a closing localized **"Follow on Instagram:" line with the full `INSTAGRAM_URL`**. Plain `•` bullets, no emoji per §5.), **ऐप को रेटिंग दें / Rate the App** (painted brass star, no state, a11y label constant "Rate the app") — the manual entry point for the rating sheet (§54): it calls `open()`, bypassing the auto-ask gate and spending no ask slot, and keeps working even after the user has opted out of the automatic prompt. Last in the group: **Instagram पर फ़ॉलो करें / Follow on Instagram** (painted ochre camera mark, state = the `@vedansh.app` handle, a11y label constant "Follow on Instagram") — `Linking.openURL(INSTAGRAM_URL)` from the same `data/shareLinks.ts`, falling back to an `Alert` naming the handle if the OS can't open it. The link is the canonical `https://www.instagram.com/…` form, **not** `instagram://`: a custom scheme would need `LSApplicationQueriesSchemes` / `android.queries` in `app.json` (a store rebuild), whereas the https URL is claimed by the installed Instagram app via universal/app links and degrades to the browser otherwise — so the row ships over OTA.
   - **जानकारी / Info** — **परिचय व अस्वीकरण** (painted information medallion; opens the pageSheet disclaimer modal with the bilingual disclaimer + "Report an Error" CTA), **त्रुटि सूचित करें** (painted report flag; `mailto` via `buildDiscrepancyMailto`), and **ऐप भ्रमण फिर देखें / Show App Tour** (painted counterclockwise arrow; a11y label constant "Show App Tour") which calls `resetTour()` to replay the first-launch feature tour on demand (§47).

**More icon alignment (October 2026).** `MoreIcon` maps every settings-row subject to the same painted family: sixteen row-specific transparent assets plus the existing lotus, compass and offering bowl. The shared utility registry also supplies a seventeenth painted question seal for Discover’s Ask Vedansh card. Regional calendars use the painted globe; widgets use four painted tiles. Every image occupies the same 30dp canvas inside a non-shrinking 38dp tile. The profile uses that same column, so its icon center and label start align with all rows despite the larger decorative disc. Chevrons have a non-shrinking 17dp column. Normal-text values are bounded to 60% of the copy column; above system fontScale 1.2, full labels wrap and values sit beneath them, while the profile summary also wraps. The bell, alarm and saved ribbon reconstruct the selected board's settings subjects; the remaining new subjects extend its palette. Generation prompts/hashes and installed before/after evidence are in `docs/evaluations/storybook-icons-2026-10-06/more-alignment/`.

**More responsiveness (Sept 2026).** Counts and navigation render without calendar calculations. `useMoreFamilySummary` runs only while MoreHome is focused: hydrate the shared tithi-only occurrence cache before solving, derive birth rules through the cooperative engine, deduplicate equal rules/dates, and request only the next occurrence. Cold scans share the background queue and cancel on blur, unmount or record changes. A timer around a synchronous annual scan is not sufficient. Optional dates may arrive after the count; failures keep the count usable. Language, reading-size and read-aloud sheets mount only while open. More stack destination screens use `getComponent` loaders so opening the hub does not evaluate every destination module; route names, parameters and back navigation remain unchanged.

**Picker sheets** — `LanguagePickerSheet.tsx` and `ReadingSizePickerSheet.tsx` are bottom-sheet `Modal`s (slide up, `modalBackdrop`, grabber, `parchmentHighlight`) following the `AddToRoutineSheet` pattern. Language lists the four `LANGUAGES` as radios each in its own script; picking one applies it (`useGitaLanguage`, §16) and closes. Reading-size shows the M/L pills + the live "श्री राम जय राम" sample (§43) + a Done button; picking a size keeps the sheet open so the preview updates. The first-run setup sheet (§47) is the same two choices in one bilingual sheet, shown once after the walkthrough.

*Removed in V4:* the tall bilingual header + `More` subtitle, the big 3-stat profile card (→ compact hero row), the inline 2×2 language grid and inline reading-size card (→ rows opening sheets), and the Panchang methodology card (it duplicated the Panchang tab, §33).

**Profile** (`ProfileScreen.tsx`) — the साधक insights surface, fed by `UserActivityContext` (reads, japam beads/rounds, per-source and per-mantra tallies, all local):

1. **Identity card** (gradient, `radii.lg`): `saffron` ॐ crest, `साधक · Sadhak` name pair, hairline, and a three-cell footer — **day streak · active days · saved verses**.
2. **Range tabs** — a segmented pill `Lifetime / Monthly / Daily`; the active tab fills solid `saffron` with `on-primary` text. An italic range caption below.
3. **Stat tile grid** — four tiles: Verses Read, Beads Chanted, Rounds (Mala), Days Active.
4. **7-day trend** — a mini bar chart (`saffron` bars on `parchment-deep`-style tracks) of daily activity (reads + beads + rounds×108), weekday labels localized.
5. Per-source and per-mantra breakdown lists for the selected range, sorted by volume, with an empty state when the range has no activity.
